Kieran Culkin Says a ‘Succession’ Spinoff ‘Would Be a Terrible Idea’: ‘What Are You Hoping For? “Cousin Greg: The Sitcom?”‘

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Sorry, “Succession” fans — Kieran Culkin is shutting down any possibility of a spinoff once and for all.

The actor, currently starring in “The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping” as a young Caesar Flickerman, joined the “Heart Breakfast” radio show on Friday to discuss the new film, though hosts Amanda Holden and Jamie Theakston also snuck in a few questions regarding the hit HBO show’s potential future. Related Stories Qatar Film Chief Hassan Al Thawadi Says World Cup Success Paved Way for Hollywood Push, Signals Next Steps on Neon Deal ‘Once Upon a Time in Harlem’ Duo Praise Neon’s Strategy to Reach Young Audiences

“I know we’re not here to talk about this, but I’m still grieving the end of ‘Succession,’” said Holden, to which Culkin responded: “Oh, you are? Get over it. It’s been three years!” When asked about a potential for a spinoff, Culkin answered, “Of course not. I think that would be a terrible idea… I mean, what are you hoping for? ‘Cousin Greg: The Sitcom?’… Yeah, that would be a terrible idea.”

And though the “A Real Pain” actor is steadfast about not retreading the show’s familiar territory, he noted that he misses playing Roman Roy and the “whole thing.”

Culkin continued: “It was fun. It was one of those where Jesse Armstrong, the showrunner, spent the entire last season trying to figure out if there was a way to do one more. I never wanted to know what was going to happen in the season and on the fourth one I did ask and when he pitched me the end of the season and I went ‘well, that kind of sounds like it’s the end of the show.’ But when he pitched me three different ideas of what season 5 could be, they all sounded good… But it ended perfectly. The way it was supposed to.

Elsewhere in the interview, Culkin divulged that he’ll be reuniting with “Succession” creator Jesse Armstrong on a new film. Culkin starred alongside Brian Cox, Sarah Snook, Jeremy Strong, Nicholas Braun, Matthew Macfadyen, Alan Ruck and more in the acclaimed drama, which ran for four seasons.

“The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping” hit theaters on Friday and brought in $56 million on opening day.

Star Trek Reboot Officially Teased as Crimson Tide Meets Training Day Sci-Fi Thriller

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Two Denzel Washington classics are strong influences on the new Trek movie

After more than a decade of waiting, it seems we may finally get a new theatrically released Star Trek movie. While dedicated Trekkies have heard that before, the directors of the only known Star Trek movie in development recently shared details about their project. The pair helming the new Trek film, Jonathan Goldstein and John Francis Daley, broke down the movie's influences in an interview with Inverse.

Specifically, they cited Training Day and Crimson Tide as jumping-off points for what they’re going for.

For those unfamiliar with Training Day, it stars Ethan Hawke as a newcomer LAPD narcotics officer under the tutelage of the corrupt detective Alonzo Harris, played by Denzel Washington. As for Crimson Tide, it’s a thriller about a U.S. nuclear submarine facing off against a Russian ultranationalist faction threatening to launch nukes at the U.S. and Japan (interestingly enough, it also stars Washington).

As for how the Training Day influence will show up in the film, Daley said the movie will center on some of the “bad apples” in Starfleet. “I would say it’s really kind of a look at the institution of Starfleet and any institution as a whole that has existed for a long time and what that does,” Daley said to Inverse. “I think the portrayal of Starfleet and the people that work within it have always been generally idealized versions of what the future could be. And while this [script] isn’t a cynical or nihilistic take on it, I think it does acknowledge that there are some bad apples in every institution that can have a strong voice and can really make an impact on that institution and everything it stands for.”

Past Star Trek movies have dealt with Starfleet bad apples before to varying levels of success, including Star Trek VI: The Undiscovered Country, Star Trek: Insurrection, and Star Trek Into Darkness. But Daly and Goldstein have another twist on the Star Trek formula.

“It takes place almost in real time, and it just is a whole other look at what a Star Trek movie could be without betraying the fundamentals of what it should be,” Daley said.

The pair added that the film will center on all-new Star Trek characters. “I mean, look, we don’t want to rely on fandom,” Goldstein said. “We don’t want to just hope that there’s enough fans of the original [TOS] or The Next Generation or whatever who will turn out for this. We want to just make a great movie that lives in the world that people are familiar with — some people are familiar with — and that others will discover. And then, with good fortune, we launch a new franchise into existence.”

Although the film is still only in development, there are reasons to be excited based on Daley and Goldstein’s recent work. The pair previously co-directed and co-wrote the surprisingly fun Dungeons & Dragons: Honor Among Thieves, which starred former James T. Kirk actor Chris Pine. Before that, they co-directed the action comedy Game Night, and their most recent theatrical release, Mayday, also received positive reviews.

Without Daley and Goldstein’s film, Star Trek would unfortunately be at risk of going on ice for a bit. Besides Star Trek: Strange New Worlds and Starfleet Academy, both of which will end after their next seasons, there aren’t any announced TV series in the works. Hopefully, this movie can get the franchise back in Paramount’s good graces.

To boldly go where the series has never gone before

Miley Cyrus Marries Maxx Morando After 5 Years of Dating

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Miley Cyrus and Maxx Morando tied the knot in Palm Springs, Calif., on Nov. 21 after five years together.

Miley Cyrus and Maxx Morando are having the ultimate party in U.S.A.

Nearly one year after the Grammy winner and Liily drummer revealed their engagement, the couple tied the knot on Nov. 21 in Palm Springs, Calif., following five years of dating, according to Entertainment Tonight.

Miley—who was previously married to Liam Hemsworth from 2018 to 2020—began dating Maxx in 2021 after they were set up on a blind date through his bandmate's sister's friend, he told Wonderland magazine in July.

As for the proposal, the couple proved that true love hit them like a wrecking ball when Maxx popped the question during a vacation in Japan, getting down on one knee in a beautiful Japanese garden.

“It was a little dead end, this beautiful Japanese forest-y situation,” Maxx told Wonderland. “She was taking videos, and was turned away. That’s when I got on one knee and tapped her on the shoulder.”

And although the “Toro” singer admitted that he was so nervous he “could feel my heart pounding,” he was able to pull off the surprise, which Miley shared wasn’t a simple feat.

“I am not easy to surprise,” she admitted during an appearance on Good Morning America following her engagement news. “I love to control every situation. I had completely surrendered. I’m telling you, I was so, so, so surprised.”

And before long, she was teasing that her and Maxx’s special day was on the horizon, releasing “Let’s Get Married” on her September 2026 album Bass Persuades, with the pair also giving an intimate glimpse into their romance in the song's music video.

“Let’s go get married tonight / Let’s get married / I’m having visions of white,” Miley sings in the track's chorus. “Let’s get married / Let’s turn the rest of our lives to a holiday / Anytime, any place, we can run away / Get married tonight / Let’s get married.”

But the couple didn't only work together on the romantic track, as Maxx additionally produced several other songs on the album.

And there’s a reason they always find their way back home to the studio, as Miley told Harper’s Bazaar in 2024, “Maxx just inspires me so much.”

But beyond their musical collaborations, the “Flowers” singer also credits Maxx with helping her with “starting over” and “building again” after she and ex Liam's California home burned down in the 2018 Malibu fires, before later separating.

“After I lost my house in the fires, that wasn't all that I lost,” Miley shared during a December press conference, per Hello! magazine. “I really actually went down to the ashes and rebuilt my life personally and physically [with] my home.”

And as Miley and Maxx enter the new chapter of their romance as newlyweds, keep reading for a look back at where their relationship began…

Miley Cyrus and musician Maxx Morando began dating in 2021. "We got put on a blind date," she recalled to British Vogue in 2023. "Well, it was blind for me and not really for him. I thought, 'The worst that can happen is I leave.'"

Miley didn't leave and, as their romance blossomed, the pair went red carpet official at the Gucci Love Parade event on November 2, 2021.

Miley and Maxx, who is a drummer in the band Liily, enjoyed a night out seeing Chicago on Broadway.

The "Flowers" singer and her boyfriend stepped out together at a Versace runway show in West Hollywood on March 9, 2023.

Maxx was by Miley's side when she won her first-ever Grammy at the 2024 award ceremony. The couple even shared a sweet smooch at their table during the event, and Miley referred to Maxx in her acceptance speech as "my love."

They returned to the Grammy Awards in 2025 for another public date night. "He’s very similar to me," Miley told Harper's Bazaar in an interview published November 2024. "We just don’t take life too seriously."

Maxx, who is six years younger than the Hannah Montana star, joined Miley on the red carpet at SNL50: The Anniversary Special, to celebrate 50 years of Saturday Night Live.

The musicians got glammed up for a big night out at the 2025 Oscars ceremony.

And later that night they stepped out in fresh looks for an appearance at the Vanity Fair Oscar Party.

It was a party in the U.S.A. when the pair got married in Plam Springs.

'SNL' Cast Members Confirm the Brutal Reality That You Don’t See Behind the Camera

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Greer Riddell is a London-based film and television journalist writing about comedy, streaming and the evolving media industry in the UK and USA. She works at Collider, covering the latest in film, television and entertainment through interviews, features, reviews and industry coverage.

Her recent brand collaborations include HBO, Warner Bros., Netflix, Hulu, Sky, Disney+ and DC Studios. She has covered titles including Saturday Night Live UK, Rivals, Scrubs, Stuart Fails to Save the Universe, Ludwig, Supergirl and Stranger Things.

Her recent interviews include Florence Pugh, Mike Faist and Christopher Abbott for Netflix's East of Eden, Taika Waititi and Jenna Ortega for Klara and the Sun.

Saturday Night Live UK writes around 35 sketches every week, but only six make it to the live show. Roughly 83% of the comedy created for an episode never reaches television, and even a sketch that makes it through rehearsals can be cut just 90 minutes before broadcast. The cast might already be in costume, the host might have performed it in front of a live audience, and the joke might have landed, but none of that guarantees it a place in the final show. If another sketch gets bigger laughs or makes for a stronger episode, the first one is out.

That ruthless selection process is central to what makes Saturday Night Live work. The show has to produce an hour of live television every week, which means there is little time to rescue a sketch that is not working or make room for one because its writers have worked hard on it. At the Edinburgh TV Festival 2026, SNL UK cast members Emma Sidi and Jack Shep, alongside Lead Producer James Longman discussed what happens to the comedy viewers never see. The result is a format in which the audience does more than watch the show — their laughter helps decide which version of the episode ultimately makes it to air.

Every Wednesday, the SNL UK team perform around 30 to 35 sketches at the table read before selecting the six that will move forward. Longman described the process as having a certain “purity” because hearing the scripts together gives the team an immediate sense of what is funny and what is not quite working. There is no guarantee that a sketch will survive because its premise sounds promising on paper — it has to compete with everything else written that week.

For the writers, that means rejection is not an occasional setback but a routine part of the job. Co-head writer Jonno Johnson explained that with more than 30 scripts to choose from, plenty of writers will inevitably find themselves without a sketch in the show. The challenge, he said, is learning “how you handle kind of failure and rejection.” That is a particularly important skill in a format that moves so quickly. Writers have only a few days to develop material, and once the team has chosen its sketches, there is little opportunity to dwell on the ideas that did not make it so resilience is key.

Cast member Jack Shep admitted at the Edinburgh TV Festival that Wednesdays can be difficult precisely because so much of the material being discarded is funny. “You do like, you know, hours and hours of hilarious stuff, and you just have to let it go,” he said. One sketch he was sad to see get cut involved a group of friends trying to persuade someone not to leave her boyfriend because he worked at Jo Malone, and they would lose access to his staff discount! Oh, and the boyfriend was a “horrific blob.”

The UK desk isn’t chasing shock value — it’s using comedy to cut through the language everyone else tiptoes around.

At 6pm on Saturday, SNL UK stages a dress rehearsal in front of a live audience, running through a longer version of the show before the 10pm broadcast. For the first time, the team can see how the sketches work as performances in front of a crowd. Longman explained that this is when they begin to feel the energy of the show, discover which jokes get laughs, and which do not. The audience's reaction becomes part of the editing process, helping the team decide what deserves to stay.

Even then, getting a laugh is no guarantee of making it to air. Emma Sidi recalled at the Edinburgh TV Festival a sketch cut after the dress rehearsal for the second episode of Season 1, hosted by Jamie Dornan. The segment was "Loose Men", a Loose Women/The View-style talk show in which Dornan blew bubbles. It had gone well in front of the audience, but Sidi admitted, “All the other sketches were way better.”

This is where the live audience becomes more than a group of people attending to purely enjoy a comedy show. Director Liz Clare explained at the Edinburgh TV Festival that the audience in the room takes precedence over home viewers because their laughter helps fuel the performance. The cast is effectively playing to two audiences — the people watching in the room and the viewers who will see the live broadcast at home. A laugh in the room gives the team useful information about the sketch, while the final decision depends on how the material fits into the episode as a whole. The audience are therefore unknowingly helping shape what millions of viewers eventually see.

After the dress rehearsal, Longman and the team gather to cut two sketches. With around 90 minutes left before the broadcast, the running order can be rearranged, material rewritten, and entire sections removed. “Everything gets scrambled, rearranged,” Longman explained. “It keeps getting rewritten, and then the show goes out of 10.” By then, a sketch can have survived the writing process, made it into rehearsal, and been performed with the host already in costume, only to disappear before the audience at home ever gets to see it. That is the brutal reality of the SNL format!

Even those at the top are not immune. Johnson recalled writing a sketch about a planet where everyone was called Paul Selby, and its inhabitants could not understand how Earth society functioned with everyone having different names. The premise seemed perfectly reasonable while he was writing it, but watching it back left him questioning the idea and his sanity!

Johnson admitted that the writers who do best on SNL UK are those willing to play with other people's ideas and abandon their own when needs must. There is not enough time to keep refining a sketch that is not working. Sidi expressed a similar attitude, explaining that writers have to be able to recognize that an idea was good and still accept that it is dead. As Longman put it, “It's all about the show. It's not about what I think is funny.”

That is the logic behind Saturday Night Live's 17% rule. SNL UK is not trying to preserve every promising idea or reward the people who have spent the longest working on it. It is trying to make the strongest possible show from the material available, using the audience's reaction as one of the tools that helps it get there. For viewers at home, the result is a show that appears to move effortlessly from one sketch to the next. Behind that apparent ease is a process of constant comparison and rejection, with most of the comedy created each week left on the cutting-room floor. Let's hope for an extended cut one day.

Saturday Night Live UK Season 2 Part 2 returns on Sky One in the UK in January 2027 and streams the next day on Peacock in the U.S.

Tom Hardy’s Sci-Fi Masterpiece With His ‘Peaky Blinders’ Co-Star Is Officially a Streaming Sensation

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

2026 was a relatively quiet year for Tom Hardy, at least until he returned to his firecracker role of Harry Da Souza in the second season of MobLand on Paramount Plus. There was concern around Hardy's future in the series when news broke that he had been fired, meaning he would not return in Season 3 of the Guy Ritchie-directed crime saga.

These reports have since been debunked, and not only has MobLand already been renewed for a third season, but Hardy is also confirmed to be back in the leading role for the next batch of episodes. In the last few years, Hardy has focused so much on his role as Eddie Brock in the Venom movies that he hasn't had as much time to star in other projects, but his future is more open now that his time as Eddie Brock is seemingly behind him.

Before starring as Venom in the SSU (Sony's Spider-Man Universe), though, Hardy had experience in some of the most epic sci-fi thrillers of the 2010s. One of the first projects that comes to mind is Mad Max: Fury Road, the 2015 sci-fi masterpiece co-starring Charlize Theron that was written and directed by George Miller.

However, several years before the release of Mad Max: Fury Road, Hardy teamed up with acclaimed filmmaker Christopher Nolan for one of the most mind-bending sci-fi epics of all time, Inception, which also stars Leonardo DiCaprio and Cillian Murphy. It's been over 15 years since Inception grossed over $830 million at the box office, and although the film doesn't have a streaming home in America, it's been in the VOD top 10 for over 300 days this year.

Inception follows a thief named Cobb (played by Leonardo DiCaprio) who has a unique skill set: He can infiltrate someone's dream and get into their head to steal corporate secrets and other valuable information. However, he must alter his approach and undertake his most dangerous job yet when a client asks him if he can plant an idea in someone's head that would shift the balance of power in the corporate world. Additional cast members for Christopher Nolan's Inception include Joseph Gordon-Levitt, Ken Watanabe, Elliot Page, and even Marion Cotillard.

Check out Inception on VOD platforms like Prime Video and Apple TV, and stay tuned to Collider for more updates and coverage of Tom Hardy's future projects.

The Top 15 LEGO Star Wars Sets To Get For Christmas, Ranked By Price

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

There's no better time to get your hands on new LEGO sets than the holiday season. Whether you're shopping for yourself or those who share a love for the galaxy far, far away, the current Star Wars offerings from The LEGO Group are particularly great heading into Christmastime.

With prices ranging from $10 to $1,000, there are some truly solid sets for practically every budget. Additionally, some of the best LEGO Star Wars sets on this list are nearing retirement, making the year-end one final shot to get your hands on some of the biggest and coolest models of all time. Likewise, many of these sets are currently offering double Insiders rewards points when purchased on LEGO.com.

Whether you're looking for smaller stocking stuffers or looking to get someone a major display piece they'll likely be building into the New Year, here are 15 of the very best LEGO Star Wars sets to get this Christmas.

The Mandalorian & Grogu's Speeder Bike (75436)

Released on January 1st, The Mandalorian & Grogu's Speeder Bike is by far one of the cheapest LEGO Star Wars models at the moment, the perfect stocking stuffer featuring two of the biggest and most popular characters of the modern Star Wars era. With a piece count of 68 and a price of just $10, it's a super solid quick build set.

Obviously, Cobb Vanth's Speeder would be the perfect companion model for the aforementioned Mandalorian & Grogu Speeder Bike set, allowing collectors to recreate scenes from The Mandalorian's second season as well as The Book of Boba Fett tie-in series featuring the live-action debut of the legendary bounty hunter Cad Bane. Complete with the yellow fins and engine that absolutely look like remnants of Anakin Skywalker's podracer from The Phantom Menace, Cobb Vanth's Speeder is a great lower-cost LEGO Star Wars option for the holidays.

327th Star Corps Clone Troopers Battle Pack (75431)

For those who love army building with their minifigures, there's no better set right now than the 327th Star Corps Clone Troopers Battle Pack, featuring 4 Republic Clone Troopers, a trio of Super Battle Droids, an AT-RT, and a dwarf spider droid. While the $44.99 price tag feels a little high for the 258-piece count, it's still a super fun battle pack nonetheless.

One of the most unique and festive LEGO Star Wars sets of all time, the Gingerbread AT-AT Walker is a must-have for those who love to decorate their homes with LEGO models. Covered in candy, lights, and frosting, complete with a cozy fireplace and wreaths in the interior, the perfect cherry on top for this AT-AT set is the incredible Gingerbread Darth Vader minifigure that comes with it.

A fully 3D build of the iconic Star Wars logo, this 700-piece model features "hidden surprises and details for fans to discover as they build," much like LEGO's similar red-and-white LEGO model. Hidden features include a mini version of Princess Leia's Tantive IV and a pursuing Star Destroyer, the very first Star Wars scene ever in A New Hope, making the model a perfect addition ahead of the franchise's 50th anniversary next year.

Andor and Rogue One's K-2SO had his own brick-built droid model, one of just a few sets inspired by the incredible powerhouse that was Andor and its two seasons on Disney+. 16 inches tall and coming with a minifigure and display plaque, the K-2SO build should look great next to LEGO's similar C-3PO models, as well as the R2-D2 and Chopper astromech builds. And speaking of Chopper...

The beloved droid from Star Wars Rebels and Ahsoka, Chopper (C1-10P) also has his own brick-built model that's still being sold by The LEGO Group. Coming in at just over 1,000 pieces, the set also comes with unique instructions allowing four people to work together and build the model at the same time, making for a great night of building during the holidays. Similarly, LEGO's Chopper set also comes with its own display plaque and is also to scale with LEGO's other droid models.

Listed as "retiring soon" on LEGO.com, it looks like the holidays will be collectors' last shot to get their hands on Battle Droid with STAP, another exciting droid model featuring just over 1,000 pieces as well. Official images also confirm that the brick-built B1 model can be displayed on its own, apart from the STAP.

Imperial Remnant AT-AT With INT-4 (75454)

Inspired by this summer's The Mandalorian & Grogu movie, the Imperial Remnant AT-AT with INT-4 model is a very cool build, featuring the entire escape craft along with 7 different minifigures. For collectors who have yet to add any kind of AT-AT to their collection, this latest opportunity is certainly a good one.

This year also saw the release of a brick-built Boba Fett, made from over 1,500 pieces, with a price tag of $170. The detail on the build is quite impressive, having peak appeal as an epic display model for any Star Wars fan who may just happen to have free office shelf space (me, I'm the Star Wars fan with office shelf space).

Netflix’s 4-Part Political Thriller Is a Masterclass in Plot Twists

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Netflix has several political thrillers worth binging, ranging from The Night Agent to Zero Day, but The Diplomat is at the top of the pack, and the numbers prove it. Now four seasons in, The Diplomat has remained consistent in its unpredictability, resulting in a major growth in viewership. With that reputation, it's no mystery why everyone seems to be tuning in, especially after Season 4's premiere.

While politics is a common subject for television, The Diplomat stands out for its in-depth look at the U.S. government, spotlighting the difficulties leaders face. By weaving together political conspiracy and personal drama, The Diplomat creates an unforgettable story full of complex characters and shocking twists. This format keeps the audience engaged and will no doubt continue to do so as the series moves into its confirmed fifth season. The Diplomat takes big swings that consistently pay off, making it a series that fans of political thrillers, layered characters, or messy relationship dynamics won't want to miss.

The Diplomat explores the challenges an international politician faces, following Kate Wyler (Keri Russell) in her role as the U.S. ambassador to the U.K. In a last-minute reassignment, Kate arrives in the U.K. just in time for a major incident. A horrific bombing on an English ship forces Kate to use her position and international connections to uncover the true culprit, but the situation is far from simple. With U.K. Prime Minister Nicol Trowbridge (Rory Kinnear) pushing for war, Kate must work quickly. As she searches for a way to stabilize the political environment and avoid greater conflict, The Diplomat keeps adding new complications for Kate and her allies.

Four seasons later, this conflict remains central to the series, showing how one situation can snowball as it tests Kate's morals. This is part of what makes The Diplomat stand out from other political thrillers. Each choice Kate makes compounds the situation, and it never goes as she, or the viewer, expects. That unpredictable narrative keeps fans invested while ensuring no two seasons end the same. The show's ability to pull off every twist it introduces makes it impossible to look away, setting the series apart in the crowded streaming environment.

As the title suggests, The Diplomat primarily focuses on politics, but it also explores the characters' complex relationships. Kate's marriage to Hal (Rufus Sewell) is central to the story as they navigate relationship difficulties. However, as they contemplate divorce, the couple is forced to acknowledge that it could hurt both their careers. Kate and Hal make an incredible team in a crisis, yet their constant arguments often undermine them, especially when they disagree about political strategy. It's a difficult situation, especially since they still have feelings for each other, though often buried beneath their annoyance. The Diplomat portrays both the strengths and shortcomings of their relationship with nuance and realism.

Four criminal masterminds. One of them was always going to be yours. Here is who comes for you, why they picked you out of everyone available, and what they want from the encounter.

Your nemesis does not want to beat you. He wants to know you completely, and he is patient enough to wait for it. The frightening part is how good the conversation is.

Your nemesis is not after money, territory or revenge. He is after a demonstration, and you are the person he has chosen to prove his point on.

Your nemesis respects you, which sounds flattering until you work out what it means. He chose you because nobody else was worth his time.

Your nemesis has no personal interest in you whatsoever, and that is precisely what makes him unsurvivable. You are not a rival. You are a stop on the way.

While Kate and Hal are at the center, they are not the only messy dynamic in the series. The Diplomat is full of compelling characters, including other couples like the unlikely pair of Stuart (Ato Essandoh) and Eidra (Ali Ahn) or, in later seasons, Grace (Allison Janney) and Todd Penn (Bradley Whitford). These relationships add a personal dimension to the story, while political alliances create further conflict, leading to some characters occasionally working against each other. These interesting elements help explain why people keep returning for each new season of The Diplomat, only cementing its reputation as one of Netflix's most stellar political thrillers.

John Carpenter Reveals Robert Rodriguez In Running To Helm ‘Escape from New York’ Reimagining

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

After 45 years, John Carpenter is still finding the right director to break an old cult classic out of lockup.

Having recently tapped Zack Snyder to write and direct a reimagining of Escape from New York, Carpenter noted he’s been considering another candidate, despite being told Synder is “on fire” during a recent podcast appearance.

“He’s not anymore, but he’s trying to get back,” Carpenter said on the Dropping Names podcast. “We’ll see. He’s a nice man. I liked him.”

The comment came after Snyder’s The Last Photograph premiered at TIFF in September to poor reviews, mostly over the film’s excessive violence and offensive content. Watch on Deadline

Carpenter added, “We have a backup: Robert Rodriguez wants to. He a new story he wants to do. So, we’ll see.”

Meanwhile, after co-writing and directing the original, Carpenter is returning to compose the score once again. “I demanded to be the composer … we’re gonna remake the score,” he noted.

The hunt for a director to helm the Escape from New York has had several candidates over the years, including Radio Silence and Neil Cross.

In Escape from New York, special forces operative-turned-prisoner Snake Plissken (Kurt Russell) has 24 hours to rescue the president of the United States from the island of Manhattan, which has been converted into the country’s only maximum security prison. The 1996 sequel Escape from L.A. followed.

9 Years Later, X-Men Finally Gives Cyclops His True Logan Redesign

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

As a franchise that has existed for 63 years and counting, X-Men is just like any other long-running comic book series in how it'll change up its core designs to fit its stories. Cyclops in particular has had plenty of revisions to his look, with cases like Age of Apocalypse, with even an Old Man Cyclops-like look in X-Men: Age of Revelation's dystopia, perfectly fitting a weary, guilt-ridden Scott Summers. But in the latest acclaimed series, Midnight X-Men by Jonathan Hickman and Matteo Della Fonte, Cyclops dons another tortured visage, with the latest issue piling on the mournful lore of the broken man behind the visor.

Midnight X-Men had already revealed the core look for Cyclops in its debut issue, but a telling flashback detailing his particular stake in a world teetering on conflict between mutants, vampires, and empyres, or mutant vampires. It also shows just how much this Cyclops has in common with Wolverine from 2017's Logan, namely, particular incidents involving the deaths of his friends. Propelled by a deep sense of self-hatred that makes all the more sense in the latest issue, Cyclops mirrors Wolverine's depiction in James Mangold's film, feeling more focused on the emotional devastation and not so much strictly on mirroring the Old Man Logan comics. And frankly, Della Fonte nails it with this latest look at Cyclops' redesign.

Already looking intentionally more gruff with a shaved head and a scruffy beard to indicate the rapid passage of time not aging him well, and not unlike the developments of Max Payne 3, Scott is not doing well in Midnight X-Men. In the stunning second issue, it lays out just what happened to the other X-Men, and why he is particularly ashamed about their fate, with the most striking scene being his most telling glimpse yet. Standing in front of a heavily cracked mirror, now shirtless, completely covered in scars, and bearing 37 hash marks on his back, Cyclops appears to carry this grim tally as a reminder of all the X-Men whom he led to their death at the hands of Jean Grey.

Originally, Scott had gone on a mission to find Storm, the original victim swayed and turned by Vlad Dracul, not fully understanding her fate. As Storm called to each of them, Jean, Iceman, Angel, and Beast were subsequently turned to empyres as well. Barely escaping with Kitty Pryde, Scott pleads with Professor X and even meets with Magneto, who ultimately don't give him the answers he wants. Instead, he rashly goes back to Jean with more and more X-Men, who are all subsequently turned, as Scott finds himself under Jean's spell, his love for her twisting him into a thrall until everyone around him is effectively torn away.

Now, he carries a reminder of this massacre, not unlike the shame and survivor's guilt Wolverine felt following Logan's offscreen Westchester Incident. One can only hope he finds similar redemption, and mercifully, a more hopeful fate than how Logan ended.

Now living as basically a vigilante and survivor ready to kill empyres practically on sight, Cyclops has a fascinating role in Midnight X-Men. One particular fellow survivor he finds, Hope, is the signal of a potential turning point on this darker path for Cyclops, even if her name is absurdly on-the-nose. Scott may have lost a more purely heroic standpoint, but he's not been turned completely evil, merely guided by trauma into survival mode. He's still a brilliant tactician, as shown by his deadly turning of the tables at Toriak Bunker in issue #1, but Hope, who is willing to help him fight back. But given his past trauma, it's understandable he'd be hesitant to lead a new generation of mutants.

Cyclops just stunned the X-Men with one line, which makes him feel less like Charles Xavier than ever and more like his counterpart Magneto.

Still, this invites another fascinating comparison to Logan, particularly its execution of Logan's bond with Laura, aka X-23. Cyclops shows himself to be a similarly reluctant guardian to her, urging her to skip town after rescuing her, but everyone knows that's not the end of it. But still, Hope represents everything Cyclops has lost, and keeping her alive will be instrumental to his redemption in this particular storyline. So as long as there's Hope, Midnight X-Men readers shouldn't count out Scott Summers just yet.

Cowboys passing attack should shine, pass rush could struggle

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Dallas Cowboys desperately need a win, and Sunday's matchup against the Tennessee Titans offers a chance to get something positive going after all the Christian Parker drama. Tennessee is 1-8 and has one of the league's worst defenses, particularly in the secondary. Dallas should have plenty of opportunities to move the ball through the air, especially with CeeDee Lamb and George Pickens coming off productive performances.

There are still reasons to be cautious. The Cowboys' defense has been a disaster this year, and Tennessee quarterback Cam Ward has improved over the past few weeks. With Dallas also changing its defensive play-calling, this matchup could tell us a lot about the team's direction. Here are two players to buy and one area to sell ahead of Sunday's game.

CeeDee Lamb has been on a tear this year, and this week's matchup against Tennessee should give him another opportunity to put up big numbers, so it's no surprise he's on this list again. Lamb is coming off a seven-catch, 72-yard, one-touchdown performance against San Francisco. That came after his 10-catch, 195-yard outing against Indianapolis.

Through 10 games, Lamb has 77 receptions for 976 yards and eight touchdowns. He's averaging 97.6 receiving yards per game and has become the most consistent weapon in Dak Prescott's passing attack. This matchup is appealing. Tennessee is allowing 266.9 passing yards per game, the most in the NFL. The Titans have struggled to contain opposing receivers, with Cleveland's KC Concepcion, Cincinnati's Tee Higgins and Jacksonville's Parker Washington all putting up big performances against them in recent weeks.

Lamb should have plenty of opportunities to take advantage of that secondary. Whether he's working out of the slot or lining up outside, the Cowboys need to make sure he's involved early and often. With eight touchdowns already on the season, there's little reason to believe his production is slowing down.

I'm not buying the Cowboys' pass rush this week, even after the unit recorded five sacks against San Francisco. That might sound strange, but Tennessee has allowed just 14 sacks this season. The concern is what Cam Ward has been doing with his legs and how the Titans have adjusted their offense to better suit him.

Ward has been much more comfortable over the past few weeks. He's completed 54 of 79 passes for 535 yards, four touchdowns and one interception across his last two games, posting a 98.9 passer rating. He's also been more involved as a runner, and Tennessee has increasingly used tempo and designed quarterback runs to create opportunities for him.

The Titans' offensive approach has changed considerably. They've incorporated more no-huddle looks and opportunities for Ward to use his mobility. Against Jacksonville, Tennessee used no-huddle on 49% of its offensive plays, the highest rate by any team this season.

That's a concern for Dallas. The Cowboys have struggled to contain quarterbacks who can escape the pocket, and their pass rush has too often lost its discipline when trying to generate pressure. If the defensive front gets too aggressive and opens running lanes, Ward has the mobility to make them pay.

There is also a new wrinkle on the Dallas sideline. Mike Pettine will take over defensive play-calling after the Cowboys removed those responsibilities from Christian Parker. Maybe the change helps, but it's difficult to expect an immediate turnaround from a defense that has struggled with execution and consistency all season.

If Lamb is the Cowboys' most consistent weapon, George Pickens might be their most dangerous big-play threat. After missing time with a hamstring injury, Pickens returned against San Francisco and delivered the best performance of his Dallas career.

Pickens hauled in 11 receptions for 145 yards and three touchdowns on 18 targets. He set career highs in catches and receiving touchdowns, giving Prescott another reliable option in a passing game that has been productive throughout the season.

Through nine games, Pickens has 50 receptions for 678 yards and five touchdowns. He's averaging 13.6 yards per catch and has shown the ability to stretch the field and win contested catches. Tennessee's secondary has struggled to contain receivers all season, and its recent performances suggest opposing offenses can find plenty of downfield opportunities. The Titans are allowing the most passing yards per game in the league, and they've surrendered several big performances to opposing receivers over the past month.

With Lamb commanding attention from the secondary, Pickens should have opportunities to exploit one-on-one matchups. The Cowboys have two legitimate receiving threats capable of taking over a game, and this is the type of matchup where both could produce.

HBO Max's 4-Part Sci-Fi Epic Is One of the Best on Any Streaming Platform

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Most science fiction franchises tend to try to reinvent themselves over the years, with mixed results. Some, like Star Wars, return with new installments that often spark divisive discourse. Others, like Blade Runner or Tron, have extremely lengthy gaps between sequels. One sci-fi franchise not only reinvented itself in the early 2010s but also delivered a truly epic scale that very few blockbusters have matched. This honor goes to the Planet of the Apes reboot series, which is available to stream on Hulu and Prime Video.

Starting with Rise of the Planet of the Apes, the new films focus on the birth of the titular Planet of the Apes, especially how the apes evolve into the dominant species on the planet. It's not the first time someone has tried to reboot the series; Tim Burton launched his own version of Planet of the Apes in 2001, and James Cameron was even approached to revive the series on film before he eventually made Titanic. The new Planet of the Apes succeeds because it takes the time to show how its world is constantly evolving, offering a sense of growth that other sci-fi franchises lack. It also doesn't hurt that one of Hollywood's best directors helped take the series to a new level.

In the original Planet of the Apes, humanity has devolved into a mute class of workers while the apes lord over them. This led to arguably one of the greatest twists in science fiction history, in which George Taylor (Charlton Heston) discovers that the "planet" he's landed on is actually Earth, ravaged by nuclear war. Rise of the Planet of the Apes shifts from a nuclear threat to a viral one, as scientist Will Rodman (James Franco) creates a drug called ALZ-112 to reverse the effects of Alzheimer's disease.

While ALZ-112 increases the intelligence of the chimpanzee Caesar (Andy Serkis), it proves to be deadly to humans, triggering a global pandemic. Director Rupert Wyatt also said that future films would explore the conflict between man and ape:

"We've incorporated elements from Conquest of the Planet of the Apes, in terms of how the apes begin to revolt, but this is primarily a prequel to the 1968 film... Caesar is a revolutionary figure who will be talked about by his fellow apes for centuries... This is just the first step in the evolution of the apes, and there's a lot more stories to tell after this. I imagine the next film will be about the all-out war between the apes and humans."

True to Wyatt's words, the next two films, Dawn of the Planet of the Apes and War for the Planet of the Apes, focused on how humanity and apes came to battle. It also brought a new director into the fold, as Matt Reeves stepped up to helm both sequels. Reeves brought a truly epic scale to the Planet of the Apes reboot, showcasing just how much the world changed in the wake of humanity's decimation. Rain-soaked forests, snow-covered terrain, and a massive military fortress are given plenty of sweeping shots, with cinematographer Michael Seresin taking time to close in on both human and apes' facial expressions — letting their eyes say much more than words could. Reeves' vision led to critical and commercial success for both Apes sequels, and paved the way for his work on The Batman.

A good score elevates a film's emotional impact. A great score affects you long after the movie's over. Here are a few examples of that excellence.

There's one person who helped make the Planet of the Apes reboot a bona fide success, and it's Andy Serkis. Serkis delivers a career-best performance as Caesar, thanks to a blend of motion capture and a truly magnetic performance. Every time Caesar is on-screen, the camera gravitates toward him, showcasing his transformation from a mere ape to a leader to a messianic figure. Even in death, Caesar continues to cast a large shadow as Kingdom of the Planet of the Apes shows how apes have taken up his words, for good and ill. It's also led the Planet of the Apes reboot to continue topping the streaming charts, showing that the new films have just as strong a hold on modern audiences as the original Planet of the Apes did on its audience.

The Planet of the Apes reboot proves that not every reboot of a popular franchise needs to rely on nostalgia or simply rehash the original story. It also showcases that there's still room for sweeping epics in the sci-fi landscape, especially as Dune: Part Three gears up to hit theaters.

It's Officially The End Of An Era For Marvel Shows

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Marvel’s ventures have been very successful in 2026, but it is now officially the end of an era for the TV side of the franchise. Upcoming Marvel movies will move the MCU back into more mainstream territory, with two Avengers films, an X-Men story, and Black Panther 3 hitting theaters in the next few years. This comes after a period in which Marvel Studios has arguably put more focus into crafting small-screen stories set in the MCU on Disney+.

Despite a somewhat mixed bag of projects releasing between 2021 and 2025, Marvel’s 2026 TV shows have been excellent. Be it Daredevil: Born Again season 2, VisionQuest, and the brilliantly unique Wonder Man in live-action or animated ventures like X-Men ‘97, Marvel Television seems to be hitting its stride of late. However, in a cruel situation involving unfortunate timing, Marvel’s TV era is seemingly at an end.

2026’s VisionQuest was a huge success in many ways; from a self-contained story about humanity and grief to a sequel to WandaVision and a stepping stone to Avengers: Doomsday, VisionQuest worked on all fronts. That said, where the former and latter points are concerned, VisionQuest does seem like the end of Marvel’s TV dominance. The show is the final one before the release of Avengers: Doomsday, and the second-to-last live-action show confirmed to be released before Avengers: Secret Wars in December 2027.

What this means is that, besides Daredevil: Born Again season 3, which is as self-contained as MCU shows can get, VisionQuest ends the Disney+ era of the franchise before upcoming movies change everything. Avengers: Doomsday will begin the end of the Multiverse Saga and, with no projects but Daredevil: Born Again season 3 set for release in the main MCU universe between it and Avengers: Secret Wars, it will likely change the multiverse drastically. Adding in the soft reboot of Avengers: Secret Wars, which will change Marvel TV forever, VisionQuest is truly the end of an era.

VisionQuest ending the current MCU TV era only makes the future of the franchise on the small screen more unclear than ever. As alluded to, Daredevil: Born Again season 3 will release in 2027, though the show has been canceled beyond that, meaning it will likely end that section of the franchise. After 2027, only one MCU TV show remains in development: Zodiac. As of right now, it is unclear what Zodiac is, what it is about, or who will be part of the series. Moreover, it is set for release after Avengers: Secret Wars' reboot of the MCU.

Other than that, Marvel Television has only standalone animated TV projects to produce, from Marvel Zombies to Your Friendly Neighborhood Spider-Man, which exist within their own continuities. All this reinforces how VisionQuest is the true end of an era for Marvel Television, as the future will only house standalone projects and those due to end soon, while the movie side of the franchise resets everything in massive ways.

Although the lack of clarity surrounding Marvel TV's future is worrying, VisionQuest concluded its most recent era in the best way possible. For one, on a broader level, VisionQuest was simply one of the studio's best Disney+ shows. Marvel TV has had as many hits as misses since 2021. The fact that the studio ended on such a high certainly makes things sweeter for Marvel and its audiences alike. Moreover, VisionQuest was a deeper, thematic conclusion for the WandaVision trilogy, which began in 2021 and has permeated Marvel TV since.

Not only was VisionQuest a good ending, quality-wise, but it signified the end of the Marvel TV storyline that started it all on Disney+ in 2021. Finally, VisionQuest's post-credit scene is one of the most exciting the MCU has produced in years, teasing Doctor Doom's upcoming rampage and the resurrection of the Scarlet Witch. This was the best way to signify the MCU's transition into the Avengers-fueled era that is about to ensue over the next year or so, only further solidifying VisionQuest as a fitting, exciting, thematically fruitful end of an era for Marvel Television.

Every Frame Of Disney+'s 27-Episode Sci-Fi Masterpiece Is A Painting

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Many shows claim to be visual marvels, but few of them deserve that title as much as Disney+'s science fiction anthology with 27 episodes and a spinoff show. As the budgets of TV shows continue to grow and the emphasis on prestige television continues to be popular, the list of the most visually stunning shows in history is constantly being updated. From beautiful animation to amazing landscapes and astonishing CGI, TV shows have only gotten more visually pleasing with time.

Some of the best shows on Disney+ also belong on that list, especially considering how vast the streaming service's catalog is. One of the most absolutely beautiful shows on Disney+, however, doesn't get the recognition it deserves. A Star Wars show, Star Wars: Visions, features some of the most breathtaking animation on Disney+ in general, to the point where every one of its frames is basically a painting.

Though it's not considered part of Star Wars canon, Visions is still well worth watching. Visions is an anthology series that focuses on showing every corner of the galaxy far, far away from a different perspective. Each of the show's 27 episodes has its own subject ranging from battles between Jedi and Sith to a punk rock band that performs for Jabba the Hutt. The most impressive part of Visions, however, is the stunning animation.

As previously mentioned, few shows deserve the title of "visual marvel" as much as Star Wars: Visions does. That's because each of Visions' 27 episodes, with a few repeats, was animated by a different studio from around the world. Volumes 1 and 3 primarily feature Japanese anime studios, but Volume 2 features animators from Ireland, India, Chile, South Korea, France, England, Spain, and more.

This Sci-Fi series from the biggest genre franchise is a masterpiece with a near-perfect Rotten Tomatoes score that could go on forever for 1 reason.

Each of those distinct studios also has their own distinct animation style. One episode of Visions, for example, is a 2D Japanese anime, another is 3D animated, and another still is stop-motion. Even within the Visions episodes that fall under the anime banner, there's still a huge amount of variety; one studio might use an ethereal inkblot style, while another uses more traditional full-color techniques. Every episode offers something unique and visually stunning.

The fact that Visions is an anthology series also adds to the visual spectacle of it. At under half an hour per episode, each studio gets to devote resources and attention they can't typically spare for an entire season of television. As such, every episode of Star Wars: Visions is essentially a short film, and every frame could be a painting of its own. The care and passion these animators had for their work drip through every second of television, and the end product is simply amazing.

One of the very few problems with Star Wars: Visions' format of featuring different animation studios and styles lies with its episode lengths. Visions episodes typically last less than half an hour, meaning a single studio and animation style will only get a handful of minutes to display their work, with a few exceptions. If you find a style, story, or aesthetic you really enjoy, there's only one or two episodes to watch.

Luckily, Star Wars recently began fixing that issue by giving Visions its first full-length spinoff show: The Ninth Jedi. Based on Production I.G's episodes from the original show, The Ninth Jedi is a direct continuation of Lah Kara's story featuring the same style of animation, albeit with a few minor updates. With eight full-length episodes, Production I.G got to expand their visual style to new planets, new characters, and thrilling new lightsaber battles.

The Ninth Jedi also highlights the sheer narrative potential of Star Wars: Visions. The story of The Ninth Jedi began as two short episodes, yet it grew into a grand tale about the dangers of apathy, the importance of resistance, and the need to discover one's darkest depths before they can reach their brightest heights. It's one of the best Star Wars shows in general, and it wouldn't have been possible if Star Wars: Visions hadn't made it possible.

What Josephine Reveals About Parenting Explained By Channing Tatum & Gemma Chan

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

After making waves in film festivals around the world all year, Josephine is finally in theaters, beginning with a limited release that expands across the country on December 4. Starring Channing Tatum and Gemma Chan, alongside a luminous performance from newcomer Mason Reeves, the film explores a family's struggles in the aftermath of a traumatic event.

Writer-director Beth de Araújo drew from her own experience to craft Josephine's narrative, telling the story through the eyes of the 8-year-old girl (played by Reeves) who witnesses a rape without fully understanding what she's seeing. Her mother Claire (Chan) recommends therapy, while her father Damien (Tatum) hopes to teach her self-defense, but neither is equipped to deal with her erratic behavior in the months that follow. To make matters worse, Josephine is called upon to testify in the trial, which makes it even harder for her to process and move on from her trauma.

ScreenRant interviewed director de Araújo and stars Tatum, Chan, and Reeves about the themes present in Josephine from its inception, the lessons its actors learned about parenting, and Reeves' favorite day on set.

ScreenRant: Beth, given how personal this story is to you, was there one element you felt had to be in there from the start?

Beth de Araújo: I really kept the focus on Josephine's opening line of, "I'm scared." Damien's last line is, "I'm scared." I always really tried to center the story every single day, just knowing about those two bookend moments, and that this is ultimately about fear. It's about sharing your fear, who you share your fear with, and making sure that we really got that.

ScreenRant: Channing and Gemma, your characters at times clash on how to best protect their daughter. What struck you most about how their bond was tested or strengthened?

Gemma Chan: When I first read the script, that was the thing that really gripped me. I didn't know what I would do in that situation. I really think these two parents both love their daughter so much, but they just don't agree on the best way to help her. And that's because of their different upbringings and the things they're bringing to this point in their lives. That was what was really compelling to me, and I think it's something that any parent could relate to.

Channing Tatum: Something that I didn't totally realize until I watched the movie is that sometimes, what works in a relationship can work at odds when you're trying to raise a kid.

I think some of Damien's qualities might be attractive to someone who has been through certain things, but then all of a sudden you put a child into the center of it, and you're like, "Wait, wait, wait, wait. I'm not sure if that's how I want to handle that." I don't think I really understood that [while filming]. I think it was a realization after for me.

ScreenRant: This is a film tackling some heavy material, but was there a day on set that you had the most fun or felt the most excited, Mason?

Mason Reeves: I think my favorite day on set was probably when everybody on set surprised me with a birthday party. When I was turning eight, I got a bunch of slime, which my mom wasn't exactly happy about. She doesn't like slime, nor does my dad. Channing got me an Instax Polaroid camera, did a little dance, and sang "Happy Birthday." It was a really funny dance.

Then Gemma got me a set of the world's smallest things. She got me a mini Jenga set, a spirograph, an Etch A Sketch, and then a mini Polaroid keychain. You can press a button, and then a plastic photo comes out, but you can't actually take photos.

Then the day after, we were going to do a piñata, but for some reason, Channing was the one that was holding the piñata, which probably wasn't the wisest choice.

Beth de Araújo: I was just scared that you were actually going to hit him or something with that stick. I was just like, "God, here we go."

Channing Tatum: If she didn't have the blindfold on, she would've hit me. Thank God for the blindfold.

Josephine is now playing in select theaters before expanding to a wide release on December 4.

An eight-year-old girl's sense of safety is sabotaged after witnessing a crime in Golden Gate Park. Try as they might, the loving adults and guardians in her life cannot find a way to comfort her or carry her through the trauma.

To complete her look, J.Lo accessorized with a few dramatic jewelry pieces fit for red-carpet royalty, including a large pendant necklace and drop earrings, both of which appeared to be set with yellow topaz diamonds. Finally, her hair was pulled back into a knotty bun and pinned with a floral hair clip, and she carried a bedazzled clutch in her arms.

Lili Reinhart Officially Moves on From ‘The Love Hypothesis’ With Mattel’s New ‘Barbie’ Replacement

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Like many of her Riverdale co-stars, Lili Reinhart has been busy since the soapy Archie Comics adaptation took its final bow in 2023. Now, she's eyeing a new role in an Amazon MGM Studios movie that the studio is hoping might be their answer to 2023's blockbuster Barbie.

Reinhart, who starred as wholesome Betty Cooper for seven seasons of the CW series, followed it up by starring in the mystery thriller American Sweatshop and the Mubi miniseries Hal & Harper. This year, she starred in The Love Hypothesis, a well-regarded romantic comedy with Tom Bateman, and the edgy horror film Forbidden Fruits. She's also been active behind the scenes, serving as an executive producer on American Sweatshop, Hal & Harper, and The Love Hypothesis.

Now, Reinhart is making her bid to play another blonde icon of the 20th century. Deadline reports that Reinhart is now circling the lead role in Polly Pocket, after previous star Lily Collins (Emily in Paris) dropped out due to a scheduling conflict; Reinhart has a prior relationship woth Prime Video, which released The Love Hypothesis. Collins, however, will remain onboard as an executive producer. The project will adapt the popular Mattel toy line of the 1990s: it consisted of a series of makeup compacts and other household objects that opened up into miniature dollhouses for the inch-high Polly Pocket figurines. It has previously been adapted into animated films and TV series, but this will be the franchise's first foray into live-action. It will also be the continuation of a relationship between Amazon and Mattel, after last summer's Masters of the Universe; the toy-based actioner disappointed at the box office, but has been a streaming smash.

Four love stories, four completely different ideas of what it means to be somebody's person. This is the one that matches how you actually fall, and what it would ask of you.

You do not fall for strangers. You fall for the person you have already been talking to for years, which is the slowest and by some distance the most durable way anybody has found to do this.

You want a partner, not an audience. Somebody who wants their own life as badly as you want yours, and who would never ask you to be smaller to make the relationship easier.

The intensity was never the problem. The secrecy is. You are drawn to a love that lives entirely in private, and to the particular ache of wanting somebody you are not supposed to want.

You do not do this cautiously. When it happens for you it is immediate and total and it rearranges everything, and you would not accept a smaller version of it.

The property has been in development for some time; Lena Dunham was originally attached to write and direct before dropping out of the project. The project's new director is actor Clea DuVall, who's previously helmed the features The Intervention and Happiest Season. The script is written by Jordan Weiss and Dan Brier, the duo behind the 2024 Kiernan Shipka rom-com Sweethearts. It will be produced by Robbie Brenner for Mattel Studios, and Reese Witherspoon and Lauren Neustadter for Hello Sunshine; executive producers include Collins, Emily Ferenbach, Jeff Waxman, and Jennifer Madeloff.

Polly Pocket is in development at Prime Video; no release date has yet been announced. Stay tuned to Collider for future updates.

‘Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping’ Hunts Down $21M in Box Office Previews

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Joseph Zada leads the cast for the sixth film in the Lionsgate franchise that last hit theaters with 2023 prequel 'The Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes.'

The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping is ready for battle in its opening weekend at the box office.

The sixth film in the Lionsgate franchise grossed $21 million in Thursday previews before hitting 4,018 North American theaters on Friday. This gives it the second-best previews figure for the franchise, behind only the $25.2 million that 2013’s The Hunger Games: Catching Fire collected and besting the $19.7 million that the original Jennifer Lawrence-led film The Hunger Games earned when launching the franchise in 2012. The first Hunger Games went on to gross $152.5 million domestically in its debut frame, while Catching Fire holds the top-grossing launch in the series by opening with $158.1 million. Related Stories Movies 'I Play Rocky' Stars Unite With Original 'Rocky' Producer for Film's 50th Anniversary Celebration Movies The Sound of Rocky: How an Anthem Was Born

Lionsgate had projected The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping to gross north of $80 million, but other sources expected that it would open much higher and potentially earn between $100 million and $130 million, which now appears the more likely outcome. Adapting author Suzanne Collins’ prequel novel that was published last year, director Francis Lawrence‘s movie carries a production budget of roughly $150 million as it revitalizes the franchise in telling a story set 24 years before the events of the original 2008 book.

In the new film, Joseph Zada stars Haymitch Abernathy, providing the backstory for the character whom Woody Harrelson brought to life in the first four movies. The cast for Sunrise on the Reaping also includes Mckenna Grace, Whitney Peak, Jesse Plemons, Ralph Fiennes, Glenn Close, Elle Fanning, Kieran Culkin and Maya Hawke.

The Hunger Games franchise’s five films have surpassed $3.3 billion at the global box office, with the series coming off of its lowest-performing entry with 2023 prequel The Hunger Games: The Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes. Starring Tom Blyth, Rachel Zegler and Hunter Schafer, that one disappointed in opening to $44 million domestically before ending its run with $166.4 million domestically and a global sum of $361.8 million.

Sunrise on the Reaping holds a 67 critical score on Rotten Tomatoes as it lags behind the franchise’s first four movies but bests the 64 percent approval for Songbirds & Snakes. In his THR review, chief film critic David Rooney wrote that the new film “delivers on emotion and brutal spectacle.”

The weekend’s competition includes Amazon MGM Studios’ I Play Rocky going wide after two frames in limited release for Peter Farrelly’s origin story of the 1976 Oscar-winning Rocky. Also fighting for attention spans this weekend is the highly anticipated video game Grand Theft Auto 6, which launched Thursday after a 13-year wait since the franchise’s last title.

The 6 Most Fun Things To Do in the First Few Hours of ‘GTA 6’

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Introducing Grand Theft Auto 6 is difficult, because there is so much to talk about, if you want to keep things broad. Broad is not the word of the day here, though. The only people who can go broad and talk about everything are reviewers who got a copy of the game some weeks early, and then the review embargo was lifted right as the game came out. Some of those people claim to have played GTA 6 for 100 to 200 hours, and many of them still claim they're finding new things. Some remain feeling overwhelmed, when it comes to talking about the whole thing and breaking down just what the game means. So, the person writing this… he’s only had the game for about two days. He’s devoted many hours of those two days to it, but he’s only about 11% of the way through the story. That does mean there won’t be spoilers here, at least as far as the narrative is concerned, but being in this position does mean only a few things can be talked about.

To spin something out of a lack of experience, though, how about this: GTA 6 is a slow-paced game at the beginning, not to the same extent as Red Dead Redemption 2, but it takes a while to really get going. But because it’s not as patient as Red Dead Redemption 2, there are still some fun things you can do once you’ve beaten the early tutorial missions (the ones that are brief and don’t allow freedom). The next few hours you spend with Jason, because the story kicks off with Lucia, the other protagonist, in prison (that was in the trailer! Not a spoiler). People have made fun of GTA 6 for having some tedious early missions, and also some activities that make you do chores and housework in preparation for Lucia getting out of prison, but alongside those slower parts, there are still some pretty wild things you can do in GTA 6 not long after you start playing the game for the first time. These are some of the most fun activities that are possible to do early in GTA 6, within the first area of the (absolutely massive) map, basically in the space between finishing the tutorial and seeing Lucia once she’s released from prison.

It’s tempting to go to Vice City early on, because that’s the big city in GTA 6. And that’s where 2002’s GTA: Vice City was set, so seeing how the city’s changed in 40 years (since that game was set in the 1980s, though if you want to get technical, they're not really in the same continuity anyway) is tempting. You can go there early, but you're not really given a reason to until later in the game, when the story takes you to other areas of the giant map. And the map is giant, because GTA 6 gives you various locations within the whole state of Leonida, which is the GTA universe’s version of Florida, so Vice City lines up with Miami. But there’s a lot to do in the first area of the game, beyond the early (and slow-paced) story missions.

There are also lots of places to go around Key Lento, but you can wander around the town for an hour or two and just get entertainment from that alone.

Key Lento is where the game wants you to stay for the first few hours, and even if it’s a small town, it’s got a lot of character and things to do. There are also lots of places to go around Key Lento, but you can wander around the town for an hour or two and just get entertainment from that alone. Some story requirements do get you interacting with some townspeople, including Mandy (pictured above), but there are things you can do by way of interacting with her and other people beyond the story that are interesting (Mandy’s memorable because of her strange dynamic with Jason, owing to him being with Lucia, but only kind of, since she’s in prison and all). And then your neighbor Shirley is an interesting character, too, asking you to get her rum and then steal a mobility scooter off another neighbor. And then there are all the other interactions you can have with NPCs who aren’t really characters, like the guy who you might see accidentally light himself on fire by spraying gasoline around at a fuel station, Zoolander-style. Just running into people around this small town is a blast, even if it’s so much smaller and low-stakes than what you'll ultimately get into later.

Well, you know, it’s a Grand Theft Auto game. There is, uh, “nightlife” to see. These parts of the game are controversial to some extent, but the first club you can go to that’s a bit risqué is a little more wholesome than similar clubs in past games. Unless you ask for a private dance, NINE1NINE is basically a normal nightclub, and going there as Jason, without Lucia, is pretty funny. You won’t have much money to spend, but you can get drunk and dance (and interact) with other people there to a greater extent than clubs in previous games. Everything is expensive, it's too loud, and you leave feeling dizzy and looking sweaty. In other words, it is a great simulation of going to an actual nightclub in real life.

It does involve venturing into Vice City, but NINE1NINE is the one nightclub you can get into without progressing through the story. And it’s a great showcase for the game’s lighting and capacity to have countless NPCs on screen at the same time. There is a real spectacle to the place, and sure, if you want to look at the risqué stuff, that option’s there as well (but not as out in the open, which is good to keep in mind if you yourself are playing GTA 6 out in the open… you know).

Like going into Vice City itself, to see a nightclub, maybe it’s better to wait a little while before doing too much by way of combat, but there are benefits to tackling some of the earlier Illegal Operations locations ASAP. If you have a gun, you can probably make some progress. Being stealthy early on helps, and then you can pick up better firearms from the first few people you take out.

Basically, it’s an adrenaline rush to take on gangs of criminals before you have many abilities and easy access to firearms. Further, if you do succeed in getting through some of these early gang hideouts, it’s the only real way you're going to find semi-advanced weapons or genuinely fast cars early on (and the latter is especially thrilling to take control of for the first time, well before any story mission lets you drive something so high-class).

This builds on that previously mentioned “meeting strangers” thing, which sure, was also a feature in previous Rockstar games, but it’s elevated in GTA 6 for sure. You can tell as much just from the early area of the game. It’s worth drawing attention to one particular encounter, though, because GTA 6 does not really waste time letting you get a glimpse of Bigfoot, or the Leonida equivalent, referred to sometimes as Skunk Ape.

He’s in the swamps around Key Lento, and look, this particular player hasn’t encountered him again, so there’s clearly more going on with him and his deal, but running into him just a few hours into the game is wild. The hunt for Bigfoot, or rumors around him being in some previous GTA games, has plagued players for years (see GTA: San Andreas, especially). But GTA 6 isn't afraid to let you see/investigate him almost right from the start. If the first 10% of the game can let you look into Bigfoot/Skunk Ape, it gets the mind racing about what weird secrets and unsettling easter eggs might be experienced dozens of hours into the game.

If the Illegal Operations side missions seem a bit daunting, one thing you should do (and get used to) is robbing places. Like, almost any building that can be entered has things you can steal in it, and there are so many more interiors than could be found in GTA 4 and GTA 5. GTA 5 did have those big heists, and you won’t be a stranger to committing crimes if you’ve played other GTA games, but the emphasis on all the smaller and spur-of-the-moment robberies in GTA 6 is something special, not to mention unique.

You do learn which kinds of stores are better to rob than others, and it’s always tense to go into a new one and scope it out, because they’ll usually have different backrooms and things like safes (and monitors where you can delete CCTV footage) in different places. You'll also be kept on your toes because cashiers react differently… like, one had a severe panic attack once, and that forces you to make a decision about helping him by getting his medical vape, or leaving him to pass out. And sometimes, you go to steal from a place and someone will already be in there trying to rob the place. For example, this player once had an NPC doing so with a banana instead of a gun, and you can embarrass him by telling the cashier that the amateur robber actually has a banana, sending the robber on the way before robbing the place for real with your gun… which is emotional whiplash for the poor cashier, but hey, it’s literally all in the game.

Driving around is what you do a lot in every GTA game, and GTA 6 being a leap forward technically makes going for aimless drives even more enjoyable and immersive. You do have to worry about fuel, but genuinely running out without a fuel station around is rare, plus you can get to most places without having to fill up. All the cars handle in interesting and different ways, and then driving is also the easiest way to get control of all the radio stations in GTA 6.

And the radio… well, yeah, like driving, listening to fake radio shows/news reports and real-world music is fun in every GTA game, but there’s a larger variety of songs here, and just an overall higher quantity of music. It would take hours upon hours of driving to hear every song, since there are more than a dozen radio stations, and some stations themselves have dozens of songs they’ll cycle through in random order. There are also all the songs written for GTA 6, and that’s impressive, plus in-game podcasts if you ever get sick of the radio stations, by any chance, but some of the most satisfying moments of the game’s first few hours involve driving and/or listening to music. Go figure.
