GTA’s Tool Choice Over Milwaukee Gives DeWalt Fans Bragging Rights

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Daniel is a lifelong gamer with a love for various genres and gameplay styles who enjoys exploring games and playing from various angles.

A big part of what’s giving Grand Theft Auto 6 its incredible hypnotic power over a statistically-significant portion of the gaming public is its gigantic, intricately-designed world. There are so, so many details to uncover all over the place if you take a moment to stop and look for them, and I’m not just talking about Bigfoot hanging out in the swamps or the many varieties of bird flying around. There are lots of little details that subtly inform aspects of the characters’ personalities and interests, including, apparently, the particular brands of power tools they prefer.

Early in the game, after receiving word that one of the game’s protagonists, Lucia, is coming back from prison, our other lead, Jason, can work to fix up their bungalow home in order to preemptively improve his relationship level with her. This means undertaking a few assorted tasks like repainting and fixing the walls, re-shingling the roof, fixing the stairs, and so-forth.

A certain subset of players couldn’t help but notice that the tools Jason uses when fixing the walls and roof are colored a distinct shade of yellow. The brand printed on the tool may be different from real-life, but fans of hardware know the distinct iconography of DeWalt when they see it.

A new report claims that over two million XBOX players lied about their nationality to play Grand Theft Auto VI a day early.

For those with no interest in power tools, DeWalt is one of the most prominent brands in the scene, manufacturing all of the usual implements like power drills, nail guns, circular saws, and all that other good stuff, usually powered by rechargeable battery packs. As is so often the case with this sort of thing, fans of DeWalt have something of an unofficial rivalry with fans of other major hardware brands, especially Milwaukee, whose tools are usually colored red.

During Jason’s various household repair chores, he uses a couple of power tools colored the same shade of yellow as DeWalt’s, both emblazoned with the logo “DEVICE.” Obviously, Rockstar can’t just use DeWalt’s actual logo in its game without its permission (though I have a feeling DeWalt would’ve loved it if given the opportunity), but the particular font that the “DEVICE” logo is printed in, particularly with the slightly smaller “E” before the “V,” is unmistakable. Looks like Jason is a DeWalt man, and fans on the DeWalt subreddit are holding this up as a victory over the likes of Milwaukee.

The particular font that the “DEVICE” logo is printed in, particularly with the slightly smaller “E” before the “V,” is unmistakable.

The particular implements that Jason uses seem to be at least partially inspired by real-life DeWalt tools. The driver he uses when fixing the walls is nearly identical to DeWalt’s 20V MAX 1/4 inch impact driver. The nail gun he uses to fix the fencing and roof doesn’t seem to have as close of a parallel, but it most closely-resembles the DeWalt 20V MAX XR brad nailer. That’s not a roofing-specific nailer, though, so either I’m wrong or Jason just doesn’t differentiate between nailer types. I’d believe either scenario.

While this may seem like a granular detail to hyperfixate on, as I mentioned, it’s just kind of fun to examine every aspect of this simulated world. In addition to what I said before about a character’s hardware preferences, it’s also interesting to hold up these simulated knock-off brands next to the real world.

A big part of the long-established aesthetic of the GTA world is riffing on aspects of real life. It’s always been a very exaggerated setting, often exemplifying the absolute worst parts of modern society for both laughs and drama. The fictional state of Leonida is pretty much Florida if it was slightly worse, and the same goes for Vice City and Miami.

GTA has also parodied more than its fair share of real-life brands and corporations over its various entries, usually with much sillier, ruder names. Sprite becomes “Sprunk,” IKEA becomes “KRAPEA,” and Twitter becomes “Bleeter,” which I am sincerely happy did not change its name to “Blex” or something equally stupid since GTA 5.

Downing Street is celebrating Rockstar's latest blockbuster as a major British achievement, but don't expect Prime Minister Andy Burnham to join the millions of players exploring Vice City.

‘Glee’ Cocreator: ‘Sadness’ Over Cory Monteith & Naya Rivera’s Deaths Will ‘Be a Part of’ Show Reboot

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

"We're still figuring a lot of it out," Ian Brennan said in an interview of the reboot rumored to include Lea Michele.

Gleeks may have been delighted when Glee‘s official social media channels announced on Oct. 21 that the musical dramedy would be returning for a reboot. Despite the good news for fans, show cocreator Ian Brennan warned that it’s not going to be all good times when the show returns.

“You’re catching these characters at a totally different part of their life,” Brennan told Variety in an interview published Thursday (Nov. 19). “The timing of these characters thinking, ‘Maybe that was the best time in my life, being in that choir in high school’ and then once you think that you’re like, ‘Oh, that show’s going to work.’”

What’s more, two core characters are obviously missing, something that would unsurprisingly play a part in the characters’ lives in the reboot. Star Cory Monteith, who portrayed Finn, died at age 31 in July 2013, while the show was between seasons. Glee would go on to pay tribute to the actor in the third episode of season five, which included the character’s death, though not a cause of death. (Monteith died after mixing alcohol and drugs, according to the B.C. Coroner’s report at the time.)

Naya Rivera, who played Santana, died in an accidental drowning in July 2020 at age 33, five years after Glee wrapped with its sixth and final season in 2015. The actress died while trying to prevent her son, Josey, from drowning while the two were boating on Lake Piru in California. The little boy, who was 4 at the time, was found safe.

“We’re still figuring a lot of it out,” Brennan told Variety of the reboot and two of the stars’ deaths. “That’s going to be part of it, that sadness and that loss. But still in all, I think [Glee] is too big of a thing to sort of let lay. There’s something about that feels right right now.”

While the TV producer believes it’s a good time to bring the show back, he admitted to the publication that he didn’t always feel that way about a potential reboot. “I wasn’t that excited about it,” he said, noting that whenever the topic came up, he’d wonder why it should even happen. “For whatever reason, in the last six months or so, I completely did a 180 about it. It just seems the idea is right. I think the time is right. Now I’m really excited about it. It’s going to be a lot of fun.”

Show creator Ryan Murphy had been teasing a potential reboot this year, even telling Entertainment Tonight in September that he had just finished the script. He shared at the time: “Brad [Falchuk], Ian [Brennan] and I, who wrote all the first two seasons, have written something.”

After the reboot received a series order by Disney+ and Hulu, reports that certain cast members were already in talks to return began to surface. Among them was Lea Michele, who portrayed Rachel Berry, though her return is not yet officially confirmed. Chris Colfer, who played Kurt Hummel, shared during an interview that he was in talks to return, while Harry Shum Jr. (Mike Chang) — who currently stars as Dr. Kwan on Grey’s Anatomy — left it a bit vague when he spoke to Entertainment Weekly in late October during his stint on Dancing With the Stars, saying he was “really, really busy.” However, the actor added, “But I would love to keep continuing because I love [Glee].”

Also among the popular Fox show’s stars — who also landed on Billboard‘s charts collectively as the Glee cast, including an impressive 207 songs on the Billboard Hot 100 — were Jane Lynch as Sue Sylvester, Matthew Morrison as Will Schuester, Kevin McHale as Artie Abrams and Darren Criss as Blaine Anderson.

Shrek 2 Remastered Fails To Capture The Magic Of Its Source Material

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

When I first heard the announcement for Shrek 2 Remastered, I couldn’t wait to get my hands on it. As I’m sure many of you can relate to, movie tie-in games were a major part of my childhood. While the rest of my family rented their favorite films from Blockbuster, I always made a beeline for the games section, where I eventually discovered one of the best licensed games from the early 2000s, Shrek 2.

To this day, I’m still a major sucker for licensed games, so revisiting a modern version of a classic wasn’t even a question. Now that I’ve played it, however, I’m left wondering if a PS5 port of the original would’ve been a better option. While this is a remaster, not a full remake, playing it back-to-back with The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time (2026) has been a jarring and eye-opening experience that highlights the right and wrong ways to bring back a beloved game from the past.

While there are plenty of games that benefit from the traditional remaster treatment of updating visuals while leaving gameplay mostly unchanged, Shrek 2 likely would’ve been better off with the reverse.

Despite their age, Shrek 2’s original graphics had their own charm, whereas improvements to character responsiveness could’ve transformed the game into a great experience for both new and returning players. Instead, Shrek 2 Remastered retains the same frustrating controls while the visuals have become something unsettling at best and downright nightmarish at worst.

Little Red, also known as Lil’ Red, was almost entirely left out of the marketing for Shrek 2 Remastered, to the point that many believed she’d been removed entirely. Now it’s a bit clearer why that decision might’ve been made. Most of the characters have a disturbing look to them now, but Lil’ Red’s makeover is certainly the most terrifying.

Characters' softer features and movie-accurate appearances have been replaced by lifeless eyes, intense contrast, and enhanced details, leaving them almost unrecognizable compared to their appearances in the 2004 release and the movie it’s based on. I'm also left wondering why so much effort was put into redesigning characters when the cutscenes haven't been formatted to fill the screen and are often cut off before they're finished.

Though the visuals were certainly disappointing, I might’ve been able to overlook them if they'd been accompanied by new gameplay, but that remains unchanged. Most stages feature four characters that a solo player or a group of players can switch between to complete the required tasks.

Nearly every character is needed over the course of a single level due to their unique abilities, preventing one player from steamrolling others through the game. However, it also means solo players will be forced to use characters with less favorable controls.

Aside from Lil’ Red, who is actually somewhat enjoyable to play, thanks to her ranged apple throws and more fluid movements, most characters have limited attack ranges and feel stiff and unresponsive. Additionally, you’ll likely find yourself swapping characters more often than necessary because trailing characters (usually Shrek) frequently get stuck on obstacles and can’t free themselves.

Perhaps the one thing still saving this remaster from being a complete disappointment for me is that I’m playing local co-op with my partner, which has been fun simply because the game is so unforgiving. On more than one occasion, we’ve had to repeat a level multiple times to the point of delirious laughter. It’s not difficult, per se, but the lack of lives and poor controls make it brutal nonetheless.

Unfortunately, all the aforementioned issues make it difficult to imagine new players getting much enjoyment out of this remaster. Some older fans may be able to overlook the subpar gameplay in favor of nostalgia and the chance to play the game again, but I am finding it difficult. I’m grateful to have the opportunity to experience it on modern consoles with my partner, but that’s where my enjoyment ends.

If you’re a new player curious about the hype surrounding the original, this remaster isn’t the way to discover it, and I’d recommend skipping it. On the other hand, if you’re a returning player eager to revisit the game and have no way to play the 2004 version, this will scratch the itch. If you do opt to play, however, I’d insist on playing multiplayer. Shrek 2 has always been better as a shared experience, but that’s especially true now in Shrek 2 Remastered.

Players do need to sign in to their Epic account to access multiplayer, which has been a major point of frustration for those playing on Steam in particular, so keep that in mind.

Overall, I’m walking away from Shrek 2 Remastered heartbroken. Had it received the full remake treatment with refined mechanics and a visual overhaul that enhanced rather than stripped away its original charm, this could have been an incredible experience for everyone to enjoy together. As it stands currently, however, I’m just left wishing I had a copy of the original game.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Yellowjackets cliffhanger: What did Natalie see?

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

A lot goes down in Yellowjackets' Season 4 premiere! The team returns to civilization in the past timeline. Meanwhile, in the present, Misty (Christina Ricci) is plotting to kill Shauna (Melanie Lynskey), while Melissa's (Hilary Swank) looking to outwit the former Antler Queen with a big move.

And yet that's not the cliffhanger that the premiere episode goes out on.

Instead, the first episode of Season 4 takes us back to the wilderness, right after Natalie (Sophie Thatcher) has successfully called for help using the frog scientists' SAT phone.

She races back to camp, shouting, "It worked! We're getting rescued." But as she reaches the team's shelters, she stops in her tracks, and says, "What the fuck?" as she looks at...something.

At the end of Season 3, Natalie conspired with Misty (Samantha Hanratty), Hannah (Ashley Sutton), and Van (Liv Hewson) to sneak away during the hunt, so she could call for help. This means Natalie wasn't present when Mari (Alexa Barajas) became Pit Girl, or for the feast that follows. The following morning is when Shauna (Sophie Nélisse) realizes Hannah has been wearing Natalie's garb to hide her absence from the ritual.

So, option one is that Natalie, who ran back to the camp not with joy but determination, was hoping to stop the hunt with the news. But she arrived to find Mari like the frog scientists had found Coach Ben (Steven Krueger): ritualistically slaughtered and cut to pieces.

Option 2 relates to the episode's opening credits. Season after season, Yellowjackets has teased details of future episodes through imagery in the title sequence. In the premiere episode, those images included an image of Teen Shauna lying on her back in the snow, badly beaten.

At the end of Season 3, Shauna was furiously crying out to know where Natalie had gone. The answer was to the mountains to use the SAT phone. So, did something happen in the camp between that moment and Nat's return that results in her shocked expression at the end of episode 1? There's only way to find out. Tune in next week to Yellowjackets.

Yellowjackets: What is Melissas plan?!

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

If three seasons of Yellowjackets has taught us anything, it's that you don't mess with Shauna Shipman (aka Shauna Sadecki). Melissa learned that the hard way last season, when she sent a tape that put the Yellowjackets on her scent.

Shauna (Melanie Lynskey) didn't just find her, attack her, and bite off a bit of Melissa's arm. She also tried to feed it to her. Well, Season 4 of Yellowjackets came back with a vengeance, and so did Melissa (Hillary Swank).

Fresh from killing Van (Lauren Ambrose), Melissa begins episode 1 on the run with her wife, Alex (Jaylee Hamidi), and their child. In a grimy hotel room, she confesses to her partner the truth (or some of it). Her name is not Kelly, it's Melissa. She was a Yellowjacket. She was on the plane that crashed.

Melissa says she "rebooted her life" — which is a wild way to reframe faking your death. "I'm still the same person you married," she insists, "just with a different name and way, way shittier past than I told you about on our first date."

Then this conversation reveals something huge:

Alex responds to Melissa's partial confession with: "My mom was out there. She's the one who called for help. She's the reason they got rescued. "

So, there were signs last season about how Tai (Jasmin Savoy Brown), Van (Liv Hewson), Shauna (Sophie Nélisse), and Misty (Samantha Hanratty) talked about Hannah (Ashley Sutton) that suggested she didn't make it out of the Yellowjackets camp alive. Alex's revelation further cements that Hannah died out there. When Alex asks her wife if she met her mom out there, Melissa lies, "No. I didn't know her."

Melissa goes on to say that it was because she wish she'd known Hannah that she moved to the late scientist's hometown to start over. "Maybe I could create a life in her honor," she says. That might be true. But even as Melissa comes clean, she's still lying to Alex. And by sharing any of this, Melissa could be putting Alex in danger from the others. At least she's honest about that.

The episode begins with the Yellowjackets in a Canadian hospital, seeing their injuries tended to. But there's no sign of Hannah.

As the Yellowjackets share their story of survival with authorities, they all recite the exact same story:

"The plane crashed. Our friends died. And the rest of starved and scavenged and prayed until they finally found us."

They claim to have no knowledge of the missing frog scientists. To cover their tracks, they let the adults assume Hannah made the distress call. So, Natalie (Sophie Thatcher) never gets proper credit for her heroics there.

How Hannah died, however, is still a mystery.

After the scene of Melissa's confession to her wife, a title card says four weeks have passed. Shauna is acting out in her frustration, lashing out at a convenience store clerk and a stinky detective, the latter of whom has lost any trace of Melissa and her family.

Cut to: Melissa and Alex setting up in a newly leased home — which is right next door to the Sadecki household. Alex seems nervous about the plan, as Melissa assures, "Hey, we can't run forever."

Then, Melissa confronts Shauna by showing up at her door with a "Hi" and a bottle of wine. To this, Shauna says, "Well, this was very stupid... You moved in next door to me. Now I know where you are."

Melissa responds, "Yeah, and everyone else will too," just as a news van pulls up behind her!

In the wilderness and in the present timeline, Melissa knows full well how Shauna's rage can result in violence, sometimes deadly violence.

However, she also knows Shauna is strategic and a survivor. By staying in plain sight, Melissa thinks Shauna will be powerless to hurt her, because to do so would bring fresh attention to the Yellowjackets and their past.

Is she right? We'll can't wait to find out.

Tracy Oliver & Issa Rae Drama ‘Open’ Lands At HBO

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: In a competitive situation, HBO has landed Tracy Oliver and Issa Rae‘s provocative drama Open for development.

The deal, which is said to include penalty, caps a lengthy sales process that started in August when the open marriage drama was taken out by Paramount Television Studios, quickly garnering seven offers.

The field was eventually narrowed down to three suitors, HBO, where Rae has a long history, PTVS sibling Paramount+, and Peacock, where Oliver has a Bel-Air spinoff in the works.

Interestingly, HBO and Paramount+ both competed for Open up until the Oct. 6 completion of the Paramount-Warner Bros. Discovery merger, which saw them become corporate siblings at the combined Skydance company, with HBO/HBO Max Content chief Casey Bloys and his team taking charge of both. HBO stayed in the race alongside Peacock, eventually winning the project. Watch on Deadline Related Stories Cancellations and Renewals 'The Gilded Age' Renewed For Season 5 At HBO Legal Michael Jackson Molestation Claimants Hit Lawyer Bryan Freedman With Malpractice Suit Over "Hush Money Agreement"

Open is co-written by Rae and Oliver a decade and a half after they worked together on Rae’s Web series Awkward Black Girl, which inspired Rae’s acclaimed HBO comedy Insecure. In Open, when a married couple who did everything “right” opens their marriage to save it, the decision sends shockwaves through their Houston friend group, igniting affairs, exposing secrets and forcing everyone to confront the lies holding their relationships together.

Oliver will serve as showrunner on the potential series, which she executive produces alongside Rae and Jax Clark via Rae’s Hoorae banner, E. Brian Dobbins for Artists First and David Becky and Jonathan Berry for 3 Arts Entertainment.

Open stems from the first-look deal Rae has with PTVS, which will serve as co-studio alongside HBO. In early 2027, PTVS is slated to merge with the larger Warner Bros. Television Group into Warner Bros. Television Studios.

The deal extends Rae’s streak of every series she has created/executive produced to date being on HBO or HBO Max, this being her first drama. She is writer and executive producer on HBO’s untitled Male Friendship comedy pilot, starring Shane Paul McGhie and Dasan Frazier.

Rae co-created, headlined and showran HBO’s Insecure, which earned 14 Emmys and a Peabody over its five-season run. She went on to executive produce HBO’s A Black Lady Sketch Show, on which she also performed, and HBO Max’s Rap Sh!t. Rae is repped by CAA, 3 Arts Entertainment, and Hansen, Jacobson, Teller.

Oliver is writing and executive producing Hilary, a Bel-Air spinoff headlined by Coco Jones as Hilary Banks, which is in development at Peacock. She also is co-writing with Scott King a romantic comedy movie for Universal, with Keke Palmer eyed to star.

With the 2017 Girls Trip, Oliver became the first Black woman to write a film that grossed more than $100 million. In TV, she created and exec produced the series First Wives Club and Harlem. Oliver also has in the works The Wedding Date, a movie for Netflix based on Jasmine Guillory’s book, Girls Trip 2, The Blackening 2, the holiday film Deck the Y’alls starring Octavia Spencer and Jessica Chastain as well as thriller drama series Paper Gods at Netflix and Not a Happy Family at Starz. She is repped by CAA, Artists First and Myman Greenspan Fox.

‘Cameron Winter at Carnegie Hall’ Review: PTA’s Spellbinding Concert Doc Is a Gorgeously Tender Performance

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

[Editor’s note: This review was originally published on October 2, 2026 during the 2026 New York Film Festival. Janus Film will release the film in limited theaters on Friday, November 20.]

Paul Thomas Anderson‘s eye for vivifying music onscreen dates back to the late 1990s when, after “Boogie Nights,” he made Technicolor mini-masterpiece music videos with his ex-girlfriend Fiona Apple — or made a San Fernando Valley-sized shrine to Aimee Mann with his third feature, “Magnolia.” Since then, he’s kicked around with HAIM while turning one third of its Los Angelino triumvirate, Alana Haim, into a screen actress. He’s shot for Radiohead and made a regular collaboration with band member Jonny Greenwood to create some of the 21st century’s most memorable scores. Many young cinephiles now associate the mischievously lilty notes of “Phantom Thread” with the poison promise of new love and obsessive creation. Related Stories Box Office: ‘Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping’ Scores $21 Million in Previews, Second-Best Result of 2026 ‘Josephine’ Review: Another Heartsick Horror Film About Real Monsters from ‘Soft & Quiet’ Filmmaker

Last December, PTA teamed up with Geese frontman Cameron Winter to film, while operating his own Panavision camera, his solo stage performance at Carnegie Hall, running through sparse piano arrangements of tracks off his solo album, “Heavy Metal.” The result, a 72-minute film now being distributed roadshow-style by Janus Films before inevitably living on the Criterion Collection, is a stunning arrangement of its own: a deeply achey, sincere but never cloyingly earnest concert movie that’s tender to the point of frailty. (Benny Safdie counts as a co-producer, and the film’s scratchy New York quality and setting can’t help but have his imprint.)

The matted-hair, lithe Winter can seem a bit uncomfortable in his own skin in the public eye, as he did onstage ahead of “Cameron Winter at Carnegie” at its New York Film Festival world premiere at Alice Tully Hall. But on the piano, laser-beamed into his songcraft, he’s quietly electrified, at seemingly at peace as his tenorous vocal glissando and nimble fingerwork on the piano produce shiveringly tender folk ballads that echo Harry Nilsson and Van Morrison. (The usual comparisons to Tom Waits and Rufus Wainright are right, too, but Winter is his own strange thing.) If you weren’t previously sold on the wily Winter antic rock chaos of Geese — rapidly becoming one of the most popular contemporary art rock bands of these times — you’re about to. Paul Thomas Anderson has created a rhapsodic shrine to the man that is nonetheless ballad-sized in its emotional scale.

Winter, in December, played and sang to a sold-out crowd of more than 2,700 at the stately cultural temple that is Carnegie Hall, and this film can at times feel like a religious experience, an incantation, a lonely vigil. The singer/songwriter was 23 at the time of the performance, one year older than Bob Dylan when he headlined Carnegie Hall, and similarly at the precipice of major fame. Fixed on a mobile rig that will at times careen and whoosh across the stage when not otherwise holding on Winter, Anderson’s camera catches the expressionism and chiaroscuro of stage lights illuminating and fading off the singer’s face.

The result is hynoptic, a concert film up there with “Stop Making Sense” but for introverts. Songs like the popular single “$0” acquire an even more wrenchingly somber quality as a lovelorn protagonist rues being exploited by a relationship (“You’re making me feel like a dollar in your hand / You’re making me feel like I’m a zero dollar man”). Stripped of production, his indie hit “Love Takes Miles” plays even better. While many of the songs sort of hover in the same key and bleed into one another, that makes for an all the more transfixing experience. The lack of an interstitial anything save for a sweet prologue and moving coda make for experience also that’s immersive, deep, and the kind whose truth you can feel somewhere in your body even if you can’t quite articulate it.

“Cameron Winter at Carnegie Hall premiered at the New York Film Festival. Janus Films will release it in theaters starting November 20.

Jennifer Lopez Wears 186-Carat Diamond Necklace to ‘JLo Show’ Premiere: ‘There’s Security All Around’

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Dolce & Gabbana piece required a four-person security detail.

Jennifer Lopez famously sings, “Don’t be fooled by the rocks that I got, I’m still, I’m still Jenny from the block.”

She certainly had a lot of rock at the Thursday night premiere of her concert film “The JLo Show.”

Lopez was not only wearing a Chantilly lace gown and a satin shawl-coat from Dolce & Gabbana, but she accessorized with jewels from the Italian fashion house, including the 186-carat diamond Sicilia necklace. The massive centerpiece yellow pear-shaped diamond alone is 110 carats. Related Stories Brad Pitt Says 'Ocean's 14' Not Shooting Until 'Late' 2027 Elliot Page and Chris Columbus Board Queer Dark Comedy ‘Callback’ as Executive Producers (EXCLUSIVE)

JLo first noticed the ensemble and the jewelry when she performed at Dolce & Gabbana’s Alta Moda fashion show in Taormina, Italy, in July. “While I was there, I saw this necklace and I saw this coat and I said, ‘I love those,’” Lopez, who is styled by Rob Zangardi and Mariel Haenn, told me on the red carpet. “He said, ‘I’m going to send them to you for your premiere.’ He gifted me the coat and he sent the necklace for tonight.”

The necklace was said to require a four-person security detail, including two armed off-duty police officers. “There’s security all around just watching the necklace,” Lopez said.

It doesn’t sound like Lopez was looking to buy the stunning piece. “This is a very expensive necklace,” she said, laughing. “It’s gorgeous. It’s beautiful. Whoever does wind up owning it will be very lucky. It’s an heirloom piece for sure.”

“The JLo Show” features Lopez in concert in Las Vegas. She performs her greatest hits while chronicling her life from being a little girl growing up in the Bronx to becoming the superstar she is today.

“I’m working on my next album, making a lot of music right now and just enjoying life right now,” Lopez said. “In a lot of ways, this show is an important show I think in my career and that it marks a certain moment in my life. It puts a button, a period, an exclamation, two exclamation points on everything I’ve done till now. Now we can tell a new story.”

“We can be a new person,” she continued. “We can go into a different part of our life that is somewhat kind of scary because I’m on my own. My kids have gone to college and I’m single and all of that, but it is also super exciting and I’ve never felt kind of better and in my own power like I talk about in the show. It’s a really good feeling and it’s a really good place to be and I’m very excited for people to experience this show … I think it will be very entertaining but also inspiring and empowering.”

Jennifer Lopez is crying "all the time" at home now that her kids have left for college."Every time I walk into the house, you can feel it. But I know it's their home. Their rooms are exactly [the same]… the doors are open. My twins are everything and both went at the same… pic.twitter.com/kcVOp3sfll

Lopez’s two children — twins Emme and Max with ex-husband Marc Anthony — are in their first year of college. “Every time I walk into the house, and you can feel it,” the singer and actress said of being an empty nester. “You can feel it. But I know that it’s their home and their rooms are exactly [the same], I don’t close the doors. The doors are open. I walk by. I see their stuff. I walk in there sometimes.”

She admits that she cries “all the time.”

“They are such a part of you,” Lopez said. “My twins, they’re everything and they both went at the same time. But they call a lot, we FaceTime a lot. Thank God. It’s a transition for them too and they realize they go away, and they go, ‘Oh, I do need Mom’ and that’s a good feeling. It has its bonuses, too.”

In an earlier interview, Lopez told me she doesn’t mind being single and isn’t looking for romance at the moment. “I don’t need to date,” she said. “I’m good right now. I’m happy. I’m not trying to fuck it up.”

After four marriages that ended in divorce and numerous boyfriends, Lopez said, “I’ve learned a lot over the past 20 years, but also the past couple of years have really been about becoming more self-aware and really understanding what love is. I had a real idea about love as a little girl and moving through this crazy life that I found myself in, I realized that what I thought love was really isn’t love.”

“The JLo Show: Live in Las Vegas” premieres Nov. 26 at 8 p.m. ET/PT on CBS and Paramount+, with an extended version available exclusively on Paramount+.

On the Couch with Leslie Bibb

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Following standout performances on series like The White Lotus, actress Leslie Bibb shares her design philosophies amid her home renovation.

Since appearing in season three of HBO’s The White Lotus in 2025, American actress Leslie Bibb has had no problem keeping busy. The North Dakota-born, Virginia-raised actress already had screen credits spanning from the Iron Man films to Apple TV+’s Palm Royale, but Bibb’s performance on Mike White’s hit series as one-third of a slightly toxic friendship trio spotlighted the actress’s knack for blending comedy and drama. Not to mention, her blunt bob served as hair inspiration for the masses. After The White Lotus, Bibb went on to guest star on HBO’s Hacks, landing her an Emmy nomination for Outstanding Guest Actress in a Comedy.

Outside her day job, Bibb also happens to be a design enthusiast—she’s currently renovating the upstate New York home that she shares with longtime partner, fellow actor Sam Rockwell. For the interiors, Bibb has teamed up with ELLE Decor A-List designer Shawn Henderson, who often pops up on Bibb’s Instagram account, attending birthday parties and surveying the concrete shell that will one day be a pool. In the kitchen, Bibb is partnering with LG Signature on appliances that gel with the new renovation.

Next up for Bibb is a starring role alongside Brett Goldstein in Amazon Prime series Escorted. She is also executive producing a show with HBO called The Rush Whisperer, a dark comedy about sorority rush. Together with the home renovation, she has trouble choosing what to highlight. “I’m just grateful that I’m able to juggle work and life and my relationship with Sam,” says the actress. For our On the Couch survey, she shares her greatest design inspirations, entertaining tips, and the artists that have her heart.

Favorite Eras of Design: All of them... well, most of them. I'm not into cold, modern pieces from the 1980s or that shabby chic stuff. But I do like a house that isn't just one thing, that is a mix of eras and has warmth—a house that is inviting and says, “Come in, kick off your shoes, grab a drink and let's hang out.” And I love color.

Favorite Sofa: I don't have a favorite sofa, but I do love an L-shaped sofa for watching TV so Sammy and I can be together but also have our own spaces to sprawl out.

Maverick design style trick: I feel incredibly lucky to have my friend and fabulous interior designer, Shawn Henderson, as my secret weapon. Home renovations can be a nightmare, but having Shawn as my partner in crime has really made the experience so much fun. And let's be honest, the kitchen is the heart of any home. When I think of LG Signature, I think about how they design to honor your lifestyle rather than compete with it.

Most beloved buildings: I love the Chrysler Building. It always takes my breath away. Also, there is a church where my parents are buried in the country and I just love it. It is so small and quaint and the view from its windows and the grounds just make me smile.

Destination Museum: Musée d'Orsay because I get to see my favorite painting by Gustave Caillebotte called The Floor Scrapers. Honestly, wandering through museums or art galleries is one of my happy places so in any city you will find me making a pit stop to get an art fix and steal some inspo. I just saw the Krasner and Pollock "Past Continuous" exhibit at the Met and it blew my socks off.

Go-to gallery or galleries: Any gallery, anywhere. Just go in and see what people are making. It will always inspire you in some way.

Favorite Artist/Artwork: I love art and wandering around a museum or gallery is one of my safe spaces. I am mesmerized by pieces from Noah Davis. I love Helen Frankenthaler and Hilma af Klint. Also, I love Nick Cave and Danny Fox and Ramon Enrich and Peter Doig. The photographs from Sally Mann I love, too. Honestly, there are so many. I am in awe.

Favorite getaway: Staycation...and hopefully at our home upstate once we are done!

Best Host/Hostess Gift: I love to wrap a bottle of olive oil in a great kitchen towel so it is two gifts in one. I like a gift that can be put to use.

Entertaining Tips: Always have a well-stocked bar with loads of options and feed your guests. Also, make sure you have a killer dance mix going because it ain't a party unless people start dancing. And if you have more than 25 people, hire a bartender. Otherwise you won't get to socialize and you will be just hosting all night and you will be exhausted.

Cocktail or Mocktail of Choice: I am obsessed with this nonalcoholic Kolsch beer by Best Day Brewing Company. And winter is upon us, so a great martini. I am in Italy right now and am besotted with Negronis.

Watch to Wear: There are two I really love: one I own that Sam gave me so long ago, my Rolex, and the other is one I want to get, which is the Cartier Baignoire.

On the Couch with Christopher John Rogers

Levi McConaughey Reveals How Dad Matthew McConaughey Shows Up for Him

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Matthew McConaughey's son Levi McConaughey shared sweet insight into their father-son bond in an exclusive interview with E! News at the Way of the Warrior Kid premiere.

Levi McConaughey is never dazed nor confused about Matthew McConaughey’s guidance.

In fact, as the 18-year-old—whose role in the Apple TV movie Way of the Warrior Kid marks his second onscreen appearance—explained, his dad shows up for him in ways that’s more than just alright.

“He's in my corner as my dad and as my friend,” Levi told E! News in an exclusive interview at the movie Way of the Warrior Kid premiere in Los Angeles Nov.19, “and I couldn't be more happy and blessed and thankful for him being a dad to me, which means a lot.”

And as Matthew watches Levi navigate his own career, the Dazed and Confused alum—who is also dad to Vida McConaughey, 16, and Livingston McConaughey, 13, with Camila Alves McConaughey—has shared very candid advice with his oldest son.

“No bulls--t in your bones,” Levi admitted. “100 percent effort. If not 100 percent, 110 percent effort.”

In Way of the Warrior Kid, Levi appears alongside Chris Pratt, Linda Cardellini, Jude Hill, Ava Torres and more—an experience he’s simply grateful to have under his belt.

“I’m just thankful to be in an environment with G [director McG] and Chris and Jude and Ava,” he noted, “and everyone to do with this movie that made it a really collaborative, creative environment. It was great.”

Matthew’s words of wisdom echoes what he’s previously shared about what he’s emphasized to Levi as he steps in front of the camera.

“You’ve got to be able to be able to detail with rejection, that’s part of it,” the Oscar winner told E! News in August. “You’ve got to hopefully love doing it and love the process so much that enough ‘nos’ don’t matter. If not, it’s not for you, because I was taught, there’s no ‘help wanted’ signs in Hollywood.”

As Matthew noted, you miss 100 percent of the shots you don’t take.

“I just try to push, to say, ‘It’s better to make a fool of yourself and find out’ then to leave an audition and go, ‘Oh I had that moment and I hesitated,’” he continued. “Make a fool of yourself. Fail more. Try and fail more. No one gives as much of a damn as you think they do.”

Way of the Warrior Kid will premiere globally on Apple TV Nov. 25.

Box Office: ‘Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping’ Scores $21 Million in Previews, Second-Best Result of 2026

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

““The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping” earned $21 million in Thursday previews and early screenings on Wednesday and is expected to dominate the box office this weekend. Analysts believe it will earn north of $100 million. The preview figures are the second-best of 2026, behind “Spider-Man: Brand New Day’s” massive $72 million haul. It’s also the second-best for the “Hunger Games” franchise, just behind the $25.5 million that “Catching Fire” earned in 2013. It’s a big improvement on the last “Hunger Games” prequel, “The Ballad of Songbirds and Snakes,” which made $5.75 million in previews and kicked off with $44 million. That film would gross over $166 million domestically and nearly $364 million globally, figures that “Sunrise on the Reaping” is on pace to easily eclipse.

“Sunrise on the Reaping” is based on Suzanne Collins’ bestselling novel of the same name. It tells the origin story of Haymitch Abernathy, the alcoholic former Hunger Games victor played by Woody Harrelson in the films. The sequel checks in with him at 16 as he’s forced to compete in gladiatorial combat. “East of Eden” breakout Joseph Zada plays Haymitch. He’s surrounded by an all-star cast that includes Jesse Plemons, Ralph Fiennes, Glenn Close, Kieran Culkin, Elle Fanning, Mckenna Grace, and Maya Hawke. Francis Lawrence, who helmed most of the series’ films, returns as director. Lionsgate, which is having a strong year at the box office thanks to “Michael,” is backing the “Hunger Games” sequel as it has the previous films.

The biggest competition for “Sunrise on the Reaping” this weekend isn’t another major movie, but the release of “Grand Theft Auto 6,” which is expected to be a phenomenon.

Why Leonardo DiCaprio's Absence From Cliff Booth's Sequel Is A Good Thing

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Katrina joined ScreenRant as a news writer in 2024, covering box office, expert interviews, exclusives, and new releases. Her work has appeared in CBR, All About Jazz, Rising Artists, and numerous other publications. She has an MFA in Creative Writing and has published more than 20 books. She currently writes for ScreenRant’s movie features team.

Brad Pitt's The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth picks up eight years after the events in Once Upon a Time... in Hollywood, where Pitt's character worked as the stunt double for Leonardo DiCaprio's Western star, Rick Dalton. Their relationship and shared journey in Hollywood were at the center of the plot.

The Cliff Booth movie is billed as a standalone sequel and spin-off, but with Rick having been a huge part of Cliff's life, it feels like something is missing without DiCaprio's return, and ever since the project was announced, there's been discussion concerning the star's potential involvement. However, he ended up not being involved in The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth.

In The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth's trailer, Pitt's character jokingly said that he no longer works for Rick because "Rick got too fat." While many fans are disappointed that there isn't a DiCaprio cameo in the movie, his absence might just give the film its biggest opportunity.

Cliff mainly worked as a stuntman during his time with Rick, but even then, the two had very different lives. Rick struggled with fading fame and being replaced by younger actors, while Cliff worked odd jobs and seemed to have a way of stumbling upon the underground scenes of Los Angeles.

Not having to work for Rick liberated Cliff and set him on a path to fully explore the broader aspects of his life. The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth took full advantage of the opportunity and saw Cliff putting his stuntman days behind him. In the sequel, he wore several hats and took on as many roles as he could handle.

From bartending to projectionist jobs and being a studio fixer, Cliff shows a side of Hollywood that the original movie wasn't able to show through Rick's perspective. Because of that, The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth is a blend of comedy and crime as Cliff gets himself wrapped up in blackmail, theft, and violent crime across the city.

Once Upon a Time... in Hollywood, at its core, is about Rick's struggle as a fading Western TV show actor in the shifting industry. The standalone sequel isn't returning to the same dynamic or repeating the same themes. Instead, it pivots into a completely new direction by focusing on the underbelly of Los Angeles.

With Fincher taking the director role, the movie is set to have a distinct tone. Known for directing iconic crime thrillers such as Se7en, The Killer, and The Game, Fincher's crime thrillers have a reputation for combining dark atmospheres and clinical precision with stories about obsession and moral collapse.

The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth is gritty and dark, and Cliff is the perfect protagonist for it. As a stuntman, he's versatile in the industry. The instability of his jobs and the lack of a defined career and identity, combined with his street smarts and background in martial arts, give Cliff the exact set of skills to navigate the underground crime world.

While some may argue that DiCaprio's Rick could potentially team up with Cliff for a misadventure in retirement, that would put the Titanic star in a role that's too similar to his retired revolutionary role in One Battle After Another.

While talking to The Hollywood Reporter, Pitt said that having DiCaprio in The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth "would have been great fun" and his potential involvement "was talked about for a while," but to have him do a cameo would require "a lot of prosthetics" and "a good couple of weeks prepping to get him."

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

How ‘Hunger Games’ Stars Jennifer Lawrence, Josh Hutcherson and Woody Harrelson Return for ‘Sunrise on the Reaping’

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

SPOILER ALERT: This article contains spoilers for the ending of “Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping,” now playing in theaters.

The mutt is out of the bag: Jennifer Lawrence, Josh Hutcherson and Woody Harrelson are back for a cameo scene at the end of “Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping,” a prequel to their mega-hit movie franchise from the 2010s.

Their returns had been leaked ahead of the movie’s release, but fans who read Suzanne Collins’ novel when it dropped in 2025 shouldn’t be surprised by their inclusion. Despite being a prequel story centering on a young Haymitch Abernathy (played by Joseph Zada), the ending of the “Sunrise on the Reaping” novel jumps ahead for an epilogue featuring adult Haymitch (Woody Harrelson), Katniss Everdeen (Jennifer Lawrence) and Peeta Mellark (Josh Hutcherson). The movie follows the plot closely, and gives fans a look at the original trio since their last outing in 2015’s “Mockingjay – Part 2.” Related Stories 'Call Me Queen,' 'Marie Madeleine' Among 10 Films Vying for Gandhi Medal at India's IFFI 'Kantara: A Legend,' 'The Kerala Story 2,' Volker Schlondorff's 'Visitation' Among 15 Films in Goa Festival International Competition

There are several other hints to the original “Hunger Games” series in “Sunrise on the Reaping,” like the inclusion of Katniss’ parents, Burdock Everdeen and Asterid March. Burdock is one of Haymitch’s best friends growing up, and Asterid is his girlfriend. They try to support Haymitch after he wins the Second Quarter Quell, but he pushes them away after suffering so much loss and turns to drinking alone in the Victors’ Village. Near the ending of the movie, Burdock and Asterid hold a baby Katniss in their arms, who locks eyes with Haymitch as he hides and watches them. Asterid was also a friend to Maysilee Donner (Mckenna Grace), who was the original owner of the famous mockingjay pin which eventually ends up in Katniss’ hands.

The “Sunrise on the Reaping” ending jumps ahead to show adult Peeta and Katniss post-“Mockingjay,” living peacefully in Victors’ Village together with Haymitch as their neighbor. They greet Haymitch and wish him a happy birthday before he goes to the meadow to pay tribute to this late love, Lenore Dove (Whitney Peak). He says hi to the geese, something he and Lenore would do as kids. It’s a touching ending that comes full circle with the original stars and Haymitch’s tragic past.

Investing - Theory, News &amp; General • Re: [2026 Boglehead] Conference experiences?

That said, I'm astonished by the above anecdote. While I concur that speakers and participants should make a conscious effort to avoid too much jargon, "ETF" is a basic building block of modern investing; if someone is not familiar with that concept, I question whether this is the right conference for them to be attending. I'm a big believer in promoting general financial literacy, but I don't think that's the function that the Bogleheads annual conference -- even "Bogleheads University" -- is intended to perform.
On the other hand, I was astonished while watching the documentary on Elizabeth Holmes and her husband Billy in the Cinema downstairs of the Casino. In particular, I was astonished that Elizabeth had never heard of Abbot and Costello, nor the famous "Who's on first?" routine. I told my wife there was absolutely no way somebody could live in the US and not have ever heard a reference to that routine. Or so I thought. Then we asked our 31 year old daughter yesterday as we passed through her town to pick up one of our dogs she had been babysitting while we were at the conference if she had heard of them, or the routine and she said no.

Dang. All those years of coaching Little League baseball where the "Who's on first?" was standard comedic routine with every team I coached. Oh well...

Again, not faulting any of the proceedings. Just providing a response of what I observed with some of the folks we met - including those at the lunch table regarding the discussion of "ETAs" (ETFs).

We had a great time at this conference. It appears the two we have attended lined up with particular stages in our own journey. The first one was while we were both in the pre-retirement phase (Chicago 2022), and this one was while both of us are now in the actual retirement phase. So we came to attend and take part in all of the sessions pertinent to being in retirement. Mission accomplished. We both absolutely enjoyed Jean Chatzky's session "Creating a Retirement Paycheck for Life". Scott Burns did not disappoint sitting in on a panel. His first gruff response had my wife turn to me and say "Who is that curmudgeon?" I laughed, and told her to just listen to what he has to say - and he had great insight about living in retirement. Great stuff! We loved that the panelists went beyond the money in talking about retirement.

The topic dealing with the centralized theme of such a high percentage of Americans being more afraid of running out of money in retirement than the fear of dying, and allowing yourself to "spend" in retirement brought up a lot of emotions for many in attendance. I know Doc Jim Dahle talks about spending in retirement a lot, so we appreciated the topic being discussed from others at the conference as well. In fact, the theme used in many presentations concerning those retired did hit home that it was okay to spend, and provided plenty of examples of how and why. I'm not saying my wife is suddenly going to allow me to click on the buy button to order a converted Sprinter camper van for $120 - $136K so we can tool around with our bikes and dogs for our cycling hobby with a van to camp and shower in, but at least the conference opened her up to the idea. It didn't hurt that we were hiking and biking with many others in Utah, Arizona, and Colorado during this trip and saw a lot of the converted Sprinter vans.

Although a new feature on Saturday via the Social Track, we did not take advantage of any of those as it was hard to be in two places we wanted to be at once. Were any of those filmed to be watched at a later date?

Kudos to all organizers and volunteers. It was really well done and covered a lot of interesting topics. I downloaded the Whova App and found it to be useful. We enjoyed meeting Andrew Bogle and talking with him, visiting with Erin Moriarty of Erin Talks YouTube channel, and enjoyed chatting with Bob Pisani who was cracking us up out in the foyer. We even got wonderful suggestions for an upcoming trip we are making in 2027 involving what to do and see in Norway from a participant. The provided food was excellent. In particular, I loved the mega-chocolate chip cookies at the break with a cup of coffee! The resort was really wonderful as we used the fitness center, sat out at the pool for some sun on Sunday afternoon, walked the surrounding area, and ate at the restaurants (big tip of the hat to Hanks), caught a movie in the cinema, and were thrilled that a Starbucks was in the lobby.

Yes, my wife lost all of her self-imposed limit of $100 in the Casino. She had fun doing so, and I emerged from the Casino smelling like a pack of smoked cigarettes and cheap cigars with my contact lenses clouded over.

10 Classic Books You Must Read Before You Die

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Apologies in advance for some omissions, but at least what’s below isn't really a ranking. All these books are essential, and they're really just 10 that are seen as that to a particularly great extent by the person behind this whole ranking. It’s not like these are the 10 most essential, nor should it be in any way suggesting that these are the only essential books everyone should read before they die.

They're good bucket list selections, though. Most are famous, and then there are a couple of slightly less well-known ones, so you could always be on your way toward getting through the lot of them (if so, congrats). Also, some of these books are long, and perhaps a bit intimidating to start, but some of the best books of all time go on for many pages and prove especially rewarding, so finding the time to chip away at these ones is very much worth it.

While it’s usually good to summarize the plot of anything you talk about and mention on a ranking like this, there are books like A Confederacy of Dunces where doing so becomes tough. This is kind of about a guy who’s searching for a job and can’t really find one, yet it’s not a very passionate search in the first place, with Ignatius J. Reilly being a far from active protagonist.

He’s unusual in other ways, too, as are all the people he runs into, with those humorous interactions being more the point of A Confederacy of Dunces, rather than a strict narrative. It’s always interesting, though, and the way John Kennedy Toole wrote this entire novel is hard to compare to much else out there, whether you're looking at other comedic novels, other books without much by way of narrative, or, honestly, just about any piece of writing out there, really.

There are many great things about Fahrenheit 451, but one of the things that stands out most is how easy it is to get through in just a day or two. It’s not a long book, and while it might not be fun in the traditional sense, since it’s about a dystopia, it is always well-paced and engaging/entertaining in other ways. And then, of course, it’s also worth pointing out how well it’s aged.

All that’s obvious. It’s obviously got timeless themes and it’s obviously not the sort of thing that wastes time or goes on for pages upon pages that aren’t really needed. Sure, it’s a little strange that even with the brief length, it has proven difficult to adapt into a film, but that does mean Fahrenheit 451 is worth highlighting as a book you must “READ” before you die, since the other ways of digesting this story don’t really do justice to what Ray Bradbury did here.

Les Misérables is famous for being very long and very heavy-going, albeit not entirely depressing, since there is humanity explored here and a bit by way of redemption and all that. There are almost two decades covered, with fictional characters existing and struggling during a tumultuous time in French history; namely, from the mid-1810s until the June Rebellion in Paris, so you get historical drama and then some more personal drama at the same time.

If you want to be kind of cheeky and/or willing to over-simplify things, you could also say Les Misérables is a novel about a man who steals a loaf of bread, and then bad things happen. There are some tangents, lots of side characters and subplots, and just a lot of stuff that feels sprawling in general, but there is plenty of room to explore everything here, and Victor Hugo’s does remain quite a joy to read (especially if translated well, of course… that could make a difference).

Just what Infinite Jest is, who knows? Maybe a psychological dramedy would be the best way to describe it? Even then, it’s kind of a mystery just because of how confounding it is, and how little certain things seem to line up with everything else. The non-chronological storytelling doesn’t help if you want to follow and understand everything, nor does the fact that there is a large cast of characters here.

Some of those characters are residents of a drug and alcohol recovery program, others are members of a tennis academy, and some other people are radicals/revolutionaries. And there’s a lot by way of family drama that relates to a good bit of that. That might make Infinite Jest sound daunting, and also, it’s got lots of footnotes and more than 1000 pages all up, but it’s a white whale kind of book that is immensely rewarding to dive into, even if it takes you a while, and even when, inevitably, you don’t understand all of it (it’s okay; no one does, just like life, maaaan).

There is something quite impressive about how much it lingers in one’s mind long after one’s technically finished reading it.

It’s a difficult book to read, and definitely not enjoyable in the traditional sense, but it’s too important to ignore, and there is something quite impressive about how much it lingers in one’s mind long after one’s technically finished reading it. It’s not a biographical book about Marilyn Monroe, but it is still about her, just more abstract, tackling her life in a way that feels like more of a psychological drama, or even a bit like a work of surreal (yet distressingly realistic) horror at times, all in service of exploring and unpacking some weighty yet continually relevant ideas/themes.

War and Peace is famously long, to the point where that's all some people seem to really know about it. That’s not helped by the length being mentioned first here, but you do have to know that going in. But it’s also the kind of thing that should be exciting, since something so well-written being this well-written for so long isn't the kind of thing that comes around all that often, you know? So maybe that supports the idea or perpetuates it, which was a careless thing to do on this writer’s part. Then again, it’s hard to convince someone to read something that’s like 1500 pages long in about 150 words, and then if you also have to summarize what the novel’s about… eh, forget it.

It is a genuinely absorbing for all those many, many pages, and while it’s hard to commit to something like this initially, dedicating oneself to the whole epic starts to feel rewarding much earlier than one might expect. Leo Tolstoy has interesting observations on history, strong opinions on Napoleon, he lets certain characters grow/change immensely, he can write good battle scenes, and he explores mortality and death more powerfully and poetically than just about any other writer not just of his time, but of all time. This massive book is truly something special, and sure, you might be scared, but you do really, really need to give it a shot.

For as admirably intense as so many other books of Don DeLillo’s are, none are as persistently uneasy and thrilling as Libra. This is one of those rare novels that doesn’t feel long enough, being a dizzying work of historical fiction that unpacks the confusion and uncertainty around the John F. Kennedy assassination, all without offering any answers, and Libra doesn’t really suggest any conspiracy theories are true or untrue.

It’s deliberately a work of historical fiction, and it’s more about uncertainty and the nature of a conspiracy (and what draws people toward them) than it is a suggestion of something that actually happened surrounding the JFK assassination. It’s such a wild and effortlessly anxiety-provoking reading experience, not to mention truly special and essential as a book, seeing as no adaptations yet exist (and if they were made, they’d probably prove very hard to get right).

The movies here are interesting (at least the 1984 one) or very good (the 2021 and 2024 movies), but Dune is one of a fair few cases where the book is superior. There is a lot of strangeness and compelling things that can be captured in one or two movies, and yeah, this is defending that 1984 one, to some extent. It’s a mess, but doing Dune in a bit over two hours was a wild undertaking and things were always going to be messy.

Sorry, bit of a tangent. But what a certain 1950s book did for fantasy, Dune did for science fiction. If you really don’t like sci-fi, it might not convert you wholly to the genre, but if you do like sci-fi and you haven’t read Dune yet, then dude, read Dune; get it done, because it’s not a dud. It’s as far from a dud as you can get, with sci-fi. And some of the sequels are interesting too, so long as you're willing for them to go into increasingly strange territory.

While The Lord of the Rings is pure fiction, rather than Les Misérables or War and Peace, which were historical fiction, the way The Lord of the Rings is written makes it feel like it happened. Like, you don’t believe these things literally happened, but it’s more a testament to J.R.R. Tolkien’s writing that you can get swept up in the world and the people in it, and also be continually impressed by just how much “history” there is to Middle-earth.

No one’s done it better, really, in terms of literary fantasy on such a scale. Maybe no one ever will, but that’s okay, because The Lord of the Rings is such a high bar and even if something scratches a slightly similar itch, that’s probably a pretty great sign. And like some other long books, finding the time for this one can be hard, but it is ultimately so worth it, and essentially essential to tackle at least once in your life.

There is quality throughout the entirety of Different Seasons, and that’s not something you can say about all the short story/novella collections of Stephen King’s. With Different Seasons, it’s all novellas, and this collection is being singled out here, rather than there being a mention of one of his bigger novels, mostly because this feels like one of the more approachable classic Stephen King books.

Like, a few people don’t like horror. And sure, you do get a bit of horror here, namely in “The Breathing Method,” but then “Apt Pupil” is more of a thriller, and “Rita Hayworth and Shawshank Redemption” and “The Body” are more dramas, not to mention tremendously moving ones at that (and they became the films The Shawshank Redemption and Stand by Me, respectively). Just really good writing here, and the stories are all easy to get through, immensely readable, and compelling in their own different/interesting ways.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

The best of Collider, directly in your inbox.

6 Shows To Watch if You Love 'Sunrise on the Reaping'

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Just under two years after the release of the book, and after much anticipation, The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping is now officially in theaters. Sunrise on the Reaping is the second Hunger Games prequel, this time focused on the 50th Hunger Games and the Second Quarter Quell. It follows a young Haymitch Abernathy (Joseph Zada), all the way through his games and the devastating aftermath.

After watching Sunrise on the Reaping, viewers will likely just want to go back and rewatch the five movies that came before it. After that, though, there are a number of excellent shows that fans of Sunrise on the Reaping should add to their lists. From other series that put young people in situations with impossibly high stakes, to shows featuring actors from the movie, these are TV shows to watch if you loved Sunrise on the Reaping.

The Wilds follows a group of eight teenage girls whose plane crashes on a deserted island on the way to an empowerment retreat for young women. Each of the girls has someone there whom they know, at least to some degree, from back home. They are all reluctant to accept that they're trapped there, but they soon have to figure out how to work together so that they can all survive and find a way out. The story is told through three timelines: the girls' time on the island, flashbacks to the events that led each of them there, and flash-forwards to their post-rescue interviews about what happened. There's just one twist that the girls don't know, though: they were brought there on purpose for an experiment, and even the plane crash was fake.

Like Sunrise on the Reaping, The Wilds follows a group of teenagers trying to survive in an environment that is not conducive to it, the main difference being that the girls on the island are working together instead of fighting each other. The Wilds also shows adults manipulating these teenagers' lives and orchestrating their suffering for their own purposes, not caring who dies or gets hurt in order to make their point. For Sunrise on the Reaping fans looking for another intense story that shows teens trying to survive in circumstances orchestrated by power-hungry adults, The Wilds is the perfect next watch.

Lenore Dove Baird (Whitney Peak) is instrumental to Haymitch's story. She believes in the possibility of a better future one day, and her hope and determination serve as an inspiration to Haymitch in even his darkest moments. For Sunrise on the Reaping fans who want to watch another Whitney Peak project next after seeing her play Lenore Dove, the Gossip Girl reboot is a great choice. In the series, Peak plays one of the main characters, a main character named Zoya Lott. Zoya is new to both New York City and Constance Billard, and she's there to get to know and have a relationship with her half-sister, Julien (Jordan Alexander).

Gossip Girl is much lower stakes than Sunrise on the Reaping, but it also centers on the complex emotions of teenagers whose lives are being manipulated by adults in power. The reboot has a compelling twist, in which Gossip Girl's identity is not kept a secret; the new account is controlled by a group of teachers who are looking for a new way to keep the students in line. As a result, the teachers cause a great deal of harm to Zoya, Julien, and their friends, whom most of the Gossip Girl posts are about.

The Society follows a group of high schoolers from the wealthy town of West Ham, Connecticut. One day, they all get on buses to go away for a school retreat, but they return that night because the road is blocked off. When they get back, though, everyone else is gone. They're the only ones left in their town, and they are unable to leave or contact anyone outside of it. Cut off from civilization, these teenagers decide to build their own society in the meantime. They call this new version of their town "New Ham," and they fight each other on how to best conserve resources and ensure their survival. They also try to investigate what happened to them and see if they can find a way to contact the outside world.

The Society, like Sunrise on the Reaping, is an intense story about teenagers trying to survive in incredibly dangerous and horrific conditions. Early on in this situation, the teens in The Society start enacting violence on one another, and multiple people die due to the disagreements caused by their conditions. The Society is an emotional and riveting series, and it is a great next watch for Sunrise on the Reaping fans looking for something fast-paced that will absolutely break their hearts.

Four of the largest communities on the planet, and four completely different reasons to belong to one. Here is yours, what it would give you, and the argument you would be having within a week.

You belong to the fandom that is really about comfort. Hogwarts works on people because it is a place rather than a plot, and the pull is not the wizard war so much as the corridors, the food, the weather and the sense that somebody is expecting you.

You belong to the deepest fandom on this list, the one where somebody has always read further than you and is quietly delighted to say so. Middle-earth rewards patience more than any other world in popular culture.

You belong to the loudest and largest tent of the four, the one where three generations are all watching the same thing and none of them agree about any of it. That disagreement is not a flaw in this fandom, it is the main activity.

You belong to the thinking fandom, the one that turns up for the argument rather than the explosion. Trek has always been a series of ethical problems in space, and the people who love it love being asked what they would have done.

For Sunrise on the Reaping fans looking to see Joseph Zada in another major role after watching him play Haymitch, We Were Liars is the perfect next watch. Based on the novel of the same name by E. Lockhart, We Were Liars is a coming of age thriller series that follows Cadence "Cady" Sinclair Eastman (Emily Alyn Lind), a teenage girl from a wealthy family. Cady has spent every summer of her life on Beechwood Island, the private island owned by her family. The series is set over the course of two summers: the present one, where Cady is trying to get back her memories from last summer after a head injury, and last summer, where everything fell apart ahead of the mysterious incident where Cady got injured.

Cady's three best friends are the rest of a group that calls themselves "the Liars": her cousins, Johnny (Zada) and Mirren (Esther McGregor), and the nephew of Johnny's mother's boyfriend, Gat (Shubham Maheshwari). Johnny deals with severe anger issues, and he keeps a number of major secrets both summers. That second summer, Johnny, Mirren, and Gat all remember what happened, but they've been hiding it from Cady at the suggestion of her doctors and her mother.

For Sunrise on the Reaping fans who want to watch another political commentary that shows people fighting to the death with the promise of eventually winning money, Squid Game should be at the top of the watchlist. The series follows Gi-hun (Lee jung-jae), a desperate man and father of a young daughter who's in serious debt and needs a lot of money quickly. To solve his problem, Gi-hun enters a competition where participants play rounds of children's games in order to eventually win prize money. The catch is, one wrong move – including losing – means getting killed, and only one person can ultimately be left standing at the end.

Squid Game is an intense and shocking series that keeps viewers on the edges of their seats from start to finish. Like Sunrise on the Reaping, Squid Game puts its protagonist in a dangerous game surrounded by people who are willing to kill both him and each other in order to win. Both Sunrise on the Reaping and Squid Game remind its viewers, though, that the real villains are the ones who set up this system in the first place.

Based on the series of novels of the same name by Kass Morgan, The 100 is a post-apocalyptic teen drama that takes place nearly a century after a nuclear apocalypse that destroyed Earth and killed most of the population. Now, society is thriving on a massive space station that orbits Earth – but it's not a perfect system, and their time left is limited. As an experiment to see if Earth is once again habitable, 100 juvenile delinquents are forcibly sent down to live there.

The 100, like Sunrise on the Reaping, follows teenagers who have been put into a dangerous situation due to circumstances outside of their control. They all just want to survive, but they disagree on how to best do that, and this often escalates to violence. Clashing that would otherwise be low-stakes costs a number of characters their lives because of these circumstances, in a way that will seem familiar to fans of Sunrise on the Reaping.

Bill Hader’s Feature Directorial Debut ‘They Know’ Acquired by Black Bear

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Black Bear has acquired the North American rights to Bill Hader‘s feature directorial debut “They Know.” Hader also wrote and stars in the horror-thriller with Linda Cardellini.

Based on an idea from Hader and his “Barry” collaborator Duffy Boudreau, “‘They Know’ follows a divorced dad who becomes increasingly suspicious and paranoid that his ex-wife’s new mysterious partner is exerting a strange, unsettling influence on their children,” according to an official logline. Further plot details and casting remain under wraps. Related Stories Jo Koy on ‘Blue in the Face,’ Steven Spielberg, Biggie’s Mom and Learning What His Name Really Means at Long John Silver’s 'Love Is Blind' Star Rob Feeley Breaks His Silence on Michaella, the Beekeeper and Why He Nearly Skipped the Reunion

Hader serves as producer with Bob Graf under his Hanarply banner. Alyssa Donovan is co-producer. MRC fully financed the film, which is currently in post-production. FilmNation Entertainment is acting as the worldwide sales agent. “They Know” marks the second collaboration between Black Bear and MRC following the studio’s acquisition of “Honeymoon/Funeral,” which premiered at the Toronto International Film Festival.

“Bill Hader is a singular talent both in front of and behind the camera, and he has delivered a film in ‘They Know’ unlike anything we’ve ever seen,” said Michael Heimler, president of production & operations at Black Bear. “’They Know’ is authentic, original and bold filmmaking at the highest level, and we couldn’t be more excited to partner with Bill and re-team with our great friends at MRC, who consistently deliver boundary-pushing films that help define their genre.”

Hader, outside of his eight-season run on “SNL,” is best known for his HBO series “Barry.” The squirmy dramedy about a contract killer with dreams of becoming an actor won 10 Emmys over its four-season run. Hader served as the star and directed several episodes. His most recent big screen bout was voicing the Cat in the Hat in the Warner Bros. adaptation of the classic Dr. Seuss storybook.

Cardellini recently took home her first Emmy for her performance in HBO’s “DTF St. Louis.” She’s also newly leading the cast of Peacock’s “Friday the 13th” prequel series “Crystal Lake” as the soon-to-be-slasher Pamela Voorhees. Her last film role was in Netflix’s family comedy “Nonnas.”

Black Bear recently released the fantasy romance “Wicker,” starring Olivia Colman and Alexander Skarsgård. The studio’s upcoming films include Guy Ritchie’s “Wife & Dog,” starring Benedict Cumberbatch, Rosamund Pike and Anthony Hopkins; David Leitch’s “Jason Statham Stole My Bike,” starring Jason Statham and Hannah Waddingham; Nahnatchka Khan’s “Honeymoon / Funeral,” starring Rebecca Ferguson and Greta Lee; “A Woman in the Sun,” starring Renée Zellweger and Sissy Spacek; “Jonah,” starring Lee and Cooper Hoffman; and “Atmosphere,” written and directed by Anna Boden & Ryan Fleck, based on the bestselling book by Taylor Jenkins Reid.

MRC recently released “The Only Living Pickpocket in New York” with John Turturro, “The Love Hypothesis” and “Wuthering Heights.” Upcoming MRC titles include “The Gallerist,” “A Place in Hell” and “Eloise.”

Deadline was the first to report Black Bear’s acquisition of “They Know.”

Here's how you do it (at your own risk - this is not an intended feature!):

The same instructions apply if you're on PS5, but it's the touchpad rather than the View button.

Bill Hader’s Feature Directorial Debut ‘They Know’ Lands at Black Bear

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Bill Hader‘s first film as a director has found its distribution partner. Black Bear has acquired North American distribution rights to “They Know,” Hader’s upcoming psychological horror film that he co-stars in with Linda Cardellini. The growing distributor, which just released “Wicker” nationwide, acquired the rights from financier MRC in what was described as a competitive situation.

Per an official synopsis, “They Know” follows a divorced dad who becomes increasingly suspicious and paranoid that his ex-wife’s new mysterious partner is exerting a strange, unsettling influence on their children.

“Bill Hader is a singular talent both in front of and behind the camera, and he has delivered a film in ‘They Know’ unlike anything we’ve ever seen,” Black Bear president of production & operations Michael Heimler said in a statement. “’They Know’ is authentic, original, and bold filmmaking at the highest level, and we couldn’t be more excited to partner with Bill and re-team with our great friends at MRC, who consistently deliver boundary-pushing films that help define their genre.” Related Stories Why Good Voice Acting Doesn’t Have to Happen in Isolation Watch the ‘Backrooms’ VFX Supervisor Explain His Unique Process of Collaborating with Kane Parsons

Hader co-wrote “They Know” with Duffy Boudreau and produced alongside Bob Graf, with Alyssa Donovan co-producing.

Hader is a three-time Primetime Emmy winner for his work on “South Park” and “Barry.” He co-created the HBO comedy “Barry” with Alec Berg and began directing episodes in Season 2, gradually working his way up to directing the entirety of Season 4. He has long been open about his cinematic influences and his desire to transition into feature filmmaking after the show ended.

Hader recently shared some more details about directing his first feature while speaking at IndieWire’s Los Angeles Future of Filmmaking Summit. He explained that “They Know” emerged after he and longtime friend and writing partner Boudreau both went through divorces and discussed their biggest fears about ways the process could have gone awry.

“We’re both divorced. We were talking about how lucky we were that we were on, like, we had really good terms with our ex-wives, and we have kids with them,” Hader said. “We started talking about the worst case scenarios of, ‘Oh, my god, this person is going through this and this person is going through that.’ And then this idea came up.”

Black Bear has yet to announce a release date for “They Know.”
