Jax Taylor experienced "complications” that have delayed his release from the hospital following kidney cancer surgery, a source close to Vanderpump Rules alum exclusively told E! News.

Jax Taylor has hit a small roadblock in his recovery from kidney cancer surgery.

The Vanderpump Rules alum's release from the hospital has been delayed due to after experiencing "some complications," a source close to Taylor exclusively told E! News Nov. 19.

"Physically, he’s very sore from the incisions," the source said, "but mentally, he’s feeling relieved to have the surgery behind him, as it’s something that’s been weighing heavily on him for months."

Fortunately, Taylor is surrounded by a strong support system, including his girlfriend Lori Krebs.

"Lori has been by his side," the source shared. "The last few months have really taken a toll on him and she’s always been someone who brings him a sense of peace. They’ve been through a lot together and there’s a very deep love and connection between them."

The insider added, "They’re truly there for one another, and I think going through something like this has only made them realize just how much they mean to each other."

Taylor, who confirmed his relationship with Krebs earlier this year, had been privately navigating his cancer diagnosis for a while before going public with his health journey. As he shared in a Nov. 19 Instagram post, “This is something I’ve kept private for the past few months because it’s been one of the scariest times of my life."

“What I thought was just a kidney stone turned into something I never imagined," the 47-year-old wrote. "My CT scan also showed a tumor on my left kidney. After many tests and appointments I found out it was cancer.”

Ultimately, Taylor underwent a five-hour surgery to have the tumor removed. While the medical crisis put the Valley star in "a really dark place," he credited Krebs for being his guiding light.

“I honestly don’t know how I would have gotten through everything without my amazing girlfriend by my side,” shared Taylor, who's dad to 5-year-old son Cruz with ex Brittany Cartwright. “She’s been my rock through all of this."

He added, "She’s not just my partner, she’s my best friend and I feel so insanely lucky to have her in my life."

A little piece of Dolly Parton was at the 2026 CMA Awards.

Nearly two months after the country music legend died, her niece Danielle Parton—who was named the brand ambassador of Dolly’s estate last month—wore a dress custom-made for the “Jolene” singer.

The all-white, fringe-forward frock was, of course, covered in rhinestones and included a trim of many colors around the low-cut neckline, bodice and skirt. Danielle, 50, paired the look with diamond jewelry and a pair of gold heeled boots at the CMA Awards Nov. 18.

“It was made for the ‘Light of Clear Blue Morning’ video that she never got to finish and get to do,” Danielle told E! News in an exclusive interview on the red carpet. “So I'm wearing it tonight in honor of her.”

And while Dolly’s niece may have been inspired by the “I Will Always Love You” singer’s wardrobe, she quickly found that her aunt Dolly’s words of wisdom have left a far greater impact.

“Almost immediately right after she passed, I gave myself one day to sit around and cry and feel pitiful about it,” Danielle continued. “Then the following day I had stuff to do, and I remember she'd always say, ‘Well, you just got to get your rear in gear and get to it.’ So that's what I did.”

But Danielle knows that Dolly’s impact goes far beyond the Parton family.

“Dolly was such an incredible human,” she said. “I'm a fan of hers as much as I am a family member, and it's always nice, especially as a family member, to see somebody in your own family have a positive impact on other people.”

As for the tribute to Dolly performed by Vince Gill and CMA host Lainey Wilson during the event, Danielle noted she was “really excited to see those that she's had such an influence on get to pay their respects in their way.”

Inside Celebrity Chef Dook Chase’s Renovated New Orleans Home—and the Dream Kitchen He Calls His Sanctuary

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Celebrity chef Dook Chase is intentionally never far from a kitchen, be it inside one of his renowned restaurants or his dream family home in New Orleans.

Chase, 44, is the fourth-generation owner of Dooky Chase’s Restaurant, the legendary culinary destination and cultural institution that’s been operating in the Big Easy since 1941. The NOLA native also runs his modern Creole eatery, Chapter IV, commuting between both businesses from a Southern-style, two-story residence that he shares with his wife, Gretchen, their daughter, Sidney, and son, Vito.

“It's a seven-minute commute to both restaurants,” Chase tells Realtor.com® in the latest Celebrity Sanctuary. “I can visit each one in its peak time. I can get a text from any server saying, ‘Hey, a table came in that really wanted to see you,’ and I [say], ‘Tell them I'll be there in seven minutes.’ I can hop in the car and get to each.”

Personally connecting with the local community over a meal is a practice that is baked into Chase’s family backstory and a tradition he proudly upholds today.

“When you think about Dooky Chase Restaurant and my grandmother, chef Leah Chase, in the time that she helmed that kitchen ... the history of our restaurant is really through the Civil Rights Movement, opening doors to everyone in the community: Blacks and whites, together, at a time when laws didn't allow it. But we always allowed it at our restaurant,” the Le Cordon Bleu-educated executive chef explains. “My grandparents, great-grandparents, [and] certainly my parents understood the importance of learning from each and every person ... and we were able to do that through food.

“One thing that I love about my family's history and what we've been able to do every Thanksgiving is, we open our restaurant on Thanksgiving Day,” he continues. “So we gather with our community first, and then we close, clean up, set the tables all over again, and then [our] families come in. It’s a way to serve others and then enjoy your day right after.”

Chase’s Thanksgiving menu is typically an “endless” buffet of classic Southern dishes, including his favorite, gumbo, followed by multiple dessert options. However, this year, the “Family Recipe Showdown” star is bringing some fresh inspiration to the table thanks to his time working with fellow chef Christina Tosi and actress Drew Barrymore on the latest “Pillsbury Bake-off Classic” competition show, due to air on Nov. 23 on CBS and Paramount+.

“We have different experiences and different backgrounds, but ... we learn from one another,” Chase says. “That's what I enjoyed about this bake-off. This really brings people together and unites them [to] think about the memories of their childhoods, their families, their celebrations, and you felt that in that room.”

In this edition of Celebrity Sanctuary, Chase welcomes a look at his hospitable kitchen where he often hosts gatherings with family and friends on ordinary and special occasions alike. Plus, he explains how the heart of the 1952-built, 4,400-square-foot, five-bedroom, five-bathroom home helps him focus on the moment he’s in as much as the meal he’s making.

Our previous home is a block away from here. I would exercise in the morning, run up and down [this] oak tree–lined street, and I would tell my wife, “If something ever comes up for sale, I'm buying.”

As I was running one morning, [the previous owner’s] children were putting up a sign, and I asked, “Can you wait a day? Don't put up the sign. Let's talk. And then if it doesn't work, put up a sign.”

They waited that day. It happened to work out. I think why it worked out was because their father understood the family atmosphere of what he wanted that next owner to be. When we shared the story of who I was and all about my family, it was like, “This is what I want to happen in this house.”

It's a beautiful house. I really enjoy it ... and am excited to raise my family and the next generation of us here.

The architectural style of this home is Southern New Orleans. We have the big oak tree. We have the brick porch. We have the wood doors. We have shutters.

I bought this home in 2009, and we moved in 2010. When we got it, it was completely gutted from the devastation of Hurricane Katrina. We renovated it and brought it to where it is today. We added on to the porch to add a new front door section where you can be greeted by artwork or by us, and then eventually get to the stairs.

One of the great things we were able to do in this house is save some of the original hardwood. The rooms upstairs that didn't have hardwood, that had carpet, we brought [the hardwood] to those rooms, refurbished them, and then downstairs, laid all new hardwood down. Most of the windows we were able to keep.

Downstairs, we wanted to open it up. Back in the day, they had walls where you would walk through a door into the kitchen, so it was closed off to the other rooms. For me as a chef, when I'm cooking, I want to see everybody. It's where people gather, so that kitchen really dictates every movement that's going on downstairs. Whether you're in the living room, the dining room, the den—you can see and smell what's going on in that kitchen.

Sanctuary, for me, it means home. It means peace. It means comfort. It's almost like you're getting a big hug that wraps you and says, “I got you.”

My comfort zone is the kitchen. It's where I find peace. That's where I can block out all the noise and really home in on what's happening right now.

The home kitchen allows you to slow down. It's different timing. There's not 200 people in the dining room waiting for their meals; there might be just three hungry people. It allows you to slow down and to really think and to really enjoy the meal that you're cooking or the meal that you're eating.

Also, we use my home as a test kitchen. We’ve done many cookbooks. Most of those recipes I've tested in my home versus tested in a restaurant, because it allows you to slow down. It allows you to measure. It allows you to take your time and say, “If I was a home cook reading this cookbook, what do I need to have?” It gives me that hybrid space of really focusing and slowing down and getting it right versus having extra things come through in and out of that kitchen. In your home, you really have that peace to enjoy the process.

We have a big six-burner stove, a griddle, two ovens, all that good stuff. We have a gray backsplash and a stainless-steel hood. A double-door refrigerator.

The countertops are like a granite marble. I have equipment on top of the countertop: We have a mixer. I love nugget ice, so there's a nugget ice machine.

The cabinets are traditional, white cabinets. They have either copper or brass [hardware]. The wall is painted a gray tone. Going into the den has a navy blue. The colors I like ... enhanced by all of my wife's tastes.

There's a center where I can move a butcher block if we want to roll out any pastry. It's open. It's not an island, but like an island. On the other side, it's a higher top where there’s almost bar seating.

My kitchen overlooks the dining room. There is a little chandelier there. Our table has windows bringing in a natural light that overlooks an oak tree.

To the left of that is a seating area that has beautiful artwork. Art is huge in my family, so we have artwork everywhere. It has four chairs and a sofa, where we have a coffee table, seating, some statues, some other creative art. Then to the right of that, it's the den and another little nook where we keep a bar.

We have enough room for people to move around. Everything has natural light, and it just flows into different rooms, but the kitchen is the center. It's the focal point of the downstairs of my house. When you have a party, the kitchen is always going to be the focal point of it. No matter what's going on in any other room, people gather in the kitchen.

We have a lot of fun for holidays, a lot of different traditions that we do here. On Christmas Eve, the family gets together and we hang till about 2 in the morning. Crazy, I know, but it's pretty cool.

My family is huge and faith [is] huge, so we certainly have a cross in there that symbolizes it’s a blessing.

I always call the people in my family that are no longer with us our guardian angels, so [I have] symbols all throughout the kitchen that's highlighting both of my grandmothers in different elements. There's a red pot that we may keep on the stove like we keep in the restaurant that my grandmother loved. We never cook with them at the restaurant. We never really cook with them at home. It was always just that showpiece, and she loved it.

Different small things that may have been from different people, whether it was my side or my wife's side, those things give you back memories of those people, so we have that all throughout the house for cherished memories and reminders that they're always with us. They never really leave us. They keep guiding us, and that's what I love about it.

I'm happy with [the kitchen] as is. I love this house. I think the only changes would be if my two kids, as they get older, they learn how to clean a little better!

But that's the beautiful thing about our home and many homes. That dining room table services as so many purposes. You're eating there. You're doing homework there. You’re having discussion there. You’re having people over there. So it does tend to get overwhelmed in terms of how it looks, but that's how important it is. That's how important food is. It's everything. It's learning. It's education. It's who we are. It's culture. It's family time. So I don't mind it.

Karli Mullane is a celebrity and entertainment journalist at Realtor.com. She previously worked at Celeb Map, POPSUGAR, and NBC, covering pop culture, lifestyle, travel, and news of all kinds. Karli earned a BA in History from UCLA and an MA in Broadcast Journalism from San Francisco State University. She is equally grateful to have reported from both Hollywood red carpets and the comfort of her Bay Area home.

‘Ramayana’ Journey Across Asia Anchors Discovery India Docuseries Marking Shashi Tharoor’s TV Hosting Debut (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Author and Indian lawmaker Shashi Tharoor is making his debut as a TV host, fronting “Itihasa: Ramayana Beyond Borders With Shashi Tharoor,” a four-part Discovery docuseries that follows the ancient Indian epic as it spread to Sri Lanka and Southeast Asia.

The series will premiere Dec. 7 in India on Discovery and Discovery+, with one new episode each week. It will be available in the English, Hindi, Tamil, Telugu, Bengali, Malayalam and Kannada languages. Related Stories Lady Gaga, Jack White, Boy George Lead Songwriters Hall of Fame 2027 Nominees How to Watch the CMA Awards (And Taylor Swift's Performance) Live Online

Each episode centers on one of the story’s four principal figures – Rama, Hanuman, Ravana and Sita – and examines how depictions of that character have changed from one culture to the next.

With India as its point of origin, the show visits Cambodia, Sri Lanka, Thailand, the Philippines, Indonesia and Malaysia to look at the shared history and cultural contact that allowed the epic to settle in each country. It also considers how regional history, religious belief and art gave rise to distinct versions of the tale, which successive generations in different places adopted and reworked.

The show arrives with the “Ramayana” already dominating global cinemas. Nitesh Tiwari’s big-screen adaptation “Ramayana: Part 1,” starring Ranbir Kapoor as Rama, Yash as Ravana and Sai Pallavi as Sita, opened Nov. 5 and grossed $147 million worldwide in its first 11 days.

“The ‘Ramayana’ is a remarkable example of how a story can cross seas and find new life in other civilizations,” Tharoor said.

Sai Abishek, head of factual entertainment, lifestyle and kids for South Asia at Skydance, said the team worked with scholars and practitioners from several countries, who also appear in the series. On-screen contributors range from historians, religious scholars and Sanskrit specialists to heritage researchers and traditional artists.

Abishek said many viewers know the Ramayana through the tradition nearest to them, “but its journey across Asia reveals a much larger cultural inheritance.”

The show is the first title under ‘Itihasa,’ a new Discovery franchise built around stories drawn from India’s past and its cultural legacy, approached from a broader perspective.

Abishek said the network was thrilled to have Tharoor on board, adding: “His understanding of history and literature, and his ability to make complex ideas engaging, will help audiences navigate the many perspectives at the heart of the series.”

Tharoor added that literature, history and cultural exchange had long been abiding interests of his, “which made this an especially fitting subject for my debut as a television host. I am delighted that this first step has been with Discovery.”

Another Disney creator deal: YouTube illusionist Zach King joins the Mouse House

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Disney continues to prove that Netflix isn’t the only competitor sniffing around YouTube‘s top talent.

For their deal, 36-year-old L.A.-based King will make 10 original short-form videos, which Disney will have exclusive rights to distribute. Disney says the agreement “will also bring [King’s] signature illusions to new content for Disney+ and [King’s] own channels inspired by Disney’s Hexed, Star Wars, and other Disney films and experiences.”

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

One of the King projects coming to Disney+ is Cardboard Wars, his 52-minute intentionally-low-budget tribute to Star Wars: A New Hope. King uploaded the film to YouTube in August, and soon will cross-post it to Disney’s streaming service.

But there’s still more: King will pop up on Disney’s social accounts as well, hosting behind-the-scenes content about the VFX and filmmaking techniques he uses in his content.

“I fell in love with filmmaking and storytelling as a young kid, grabbing my parents’ home video camera and teaching myself to edit,” King wrote in an Instagram post. “A lot of my early homemade films were inspired by the Disney movies and the behind-the-scenes footage they would show in the DVD featurettes. There was always a sense of magic I would see on the screen, and so I’m thrilled to be working with Disney to continue that magic of filmmaking for the next generation of storytellers.”

King has worked with Disney regularly in recent months: He was at its glitzy Created in L.A. event in September, and earlier this month played a game of viral format Cake or Fake? with Hexed (and Arcane, and Sinners, and Spider-Verse, and Hawkeye) star Hailee Steinfeld. But this is his first official, expansive content deal.

“Zach makes the impossible look real, bringing the kind of imagination and originality that has always defined Disney,” Asad Ayaz, The Walt Disney Company’s CMO and Brand Officer, said in a statement. “He brings both a filmmaker’s ingenuity and a fan’s passion to our stories, along with a unique connection with audiences. We want to build lasting relationships with creators like Zach, giving their ideas room to grow across Disney. That’s what makes this next chapter of our work together so full of possibility.”

As we mentioned above, everyone–including YouTube–has had their eye on Netflix’s obvious poaching efforts…leaving room for Disney to sneak in the back.

In the last couple months, Disney has announced deals with major creators Dhar Mann, Dude Perfect, and Like Nastya. Crucially, these are all development deals–the exact kind of agreement that has YouTube on guard with Netflix.

And Disney’s not quite showing its full hand just yet. During its earnings call Nov. 17, executives only mentioned YouTubers once, in passing and in reference to Disney+’s growing crop of approved creator content.

We suspect more is in the pipeline–and that YouTube may have to start paying creators to turn down Disney deals, too.

5 Great LitRPG Novels That Are Nothing Like ‘Dungeon Crawler Carl’

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The LitRPG genre (short for literary role-playing game) has taken off like a rocket over the last few years. LitRPG has been around for decades, but the emergence of Matt Dinniman's bestselling Dungeon Crawler Carl series has seen it move from an underground niche to a genre with dedicated shelves at chain book stores. Dungeon Crawler Carl has the zeitgeist in a way we haven't seen in some time, and it is paving the way for countless others. We're already seeing other hits like He Who Fights With Monsters appear in places like Target and Walmart, signaling the LitRPG genre is continuing to expand its footprint.

One common misconception, however, is that Dungeon Crawler Carl is totally representative of the wider LitRPG genre. While the entire series is fantastic, it's a small slice of what is a surprisingly large genre pie. There are so many hidden gems within progression sci-fi and fantasy, and Dungeon Crawler Carl is just the tip of the iceberg.

So if you enjoyed your time in the dungeon with Carl and Donut, but you're looking to see what else LitRPG has to offer, here's a list of five awesome stories in the genre that are guaranteed to take you in completely different directions.

Matt Dinniman has often described Dungeon Crawler Carl as a horror story, and it's not difficult to understand why. The bigger universe epic happening throughout the series is haunting at times, and there are plenty of body horror motifs stuffed into the dungeon itself. That said, Dungeon Crawler Carl doesn't necessarily fit into the traditional horror genre box, but fans wanting old-school scares and classic horror archetypes do have another series they can turn to.

Rob M. Lastrel's The Game at Carousel series is a horror movie molded into LitRPG form. The first book, The Bystander, sees a group of friends arrive in the small town of Carousel, only to be brought into a survival game in which they all take on the tropes of horror movie characters. Those archetypes, including the Film Buff and Final Girl, evolve as the friends play into their roles, and they have to figure out how to use those tropes to survive an onslaught of killers.

This isn't the first time Tree of Aeons has come up here, and it probably won't be the last. That's because the concept of this book feels deliriously unhinged, but spaizzzer turns it into one of the most thoughtful and compelling narratives within the LitRPG genre.

The eight-book series follows a protagonist that is reborn into a vast world of magic and fantasy. Instead of becoming a great warrior or skilled sorcerer, however, Matt is reborn into this world as a tree. That's right, the main character is literally a tree, and the story couldn't be more enjoyable. As a tree, Matt finds himself amidst an ongoing war with demons, and he must figure out how to level up his seemingly limited abilities to help the warriors around him.

It lacks the bombastic energy of a series like Dungeon Crawler Carl, but Tree of Aeons replaces it with introspection and ingenuity. Not only is this series unlike Dungeon Crawler Carl, it's unlike pretty much anything you've ever read.

While it does share the similar comedic trappings of Dungeon Crawler Carl, A.P. Scribe's hit series is an adventure of a completely different scale. Instead of saving the entire human race by working through increasingly deadly levels of a galactic reality TV show, Dungeons & Deliveries is about a regular guy just trying to deliver pizzas on time.

In a fantastical version of Toronto, protagonist Alex is struggling to get by. Unable to get hired by any guilds or adventuring parties, he takes on a job delivering pizzas. Unfortunately for him, many of his clients turn out to be dungeon bosses, and he has to dive deep into their dungeons to get them their pies, trying hard just to make it back to work in one piece.

Hopping through worlds with a New York plumber makes for a fun and familiar adventure.

There are slice-of-life progression fantasy stories, which take away the massive stakes of books like Dungeon Crawler Carl and focus on the more everyday lives of characters instead. Then there's Heretical Fishing, which goes so much further than many of those other titles, creating what might be the heaviest "vibes only" saga in all of LitRPG.

It may not be for everyone, but reading Jobson's Heretical Fishing series often feels like sitting out on the peer and listening to the soft sounds of the water as a cool breeze blows against your face. It's as relaxing an experience as you'll find in LitRPG, and it stands out amongst the crowd for all the ways it forces both its characters and you to slow down a little bit.

If you want something truly out of left field, and not about a sentient tree, then you may want to turn to Cassius and Tang's Mimic and Me series. This hilarious and heartfelt saga is about a protagonist who is left on death's door at the very beginning of the series, and only remains alive when a seemingly monstrous mimic stumbles across him. The mimic ends up bonding with the character and, from there, it turns into a Venom-esque buddy trip through a LitRPG world.

The story is nothing like Dungeon Crawler Carl, but fans of the famous DCC audiobooks will be keen to check out those versions of Mimic and Me, as Jeff Hayes is a featured voice in the productions. Ryan H. Reid is the main narrator for the audiobooks and Dorrie Sacks is featured alongside Hayes.

Avengers: Doomsday's New Runtime Report Is Disappointing For 1 Key Reason

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Avengers: Doomsday's runtime has reportedly been confirmed, and it comes with a disappointing update for one key reason. Fans have been waiting for a new Avengers movie for seven years. Honestly, the Marvel Cinematic Universe dropped the ball by not having an Avengers film in Phase 4, as that would have set up the Multiverse Saga in a major way, making new heroes important.

As it stands, several heroes introduced during the latest chapter of the shared universe have only appeared once so far and/or not interacted with characters from other corners of the MCU. Instead of making a smaller, controversial TV show out of the Secret Invasion storyline, Marvel could have easily used it for a grounded and intense Avengers movie to establish a new team.

That did not happen, but the MCU is now headed to one of the biggest events ever with the release of Avengers: Doomsday in theaters on December 18, a month from now. The upcoming Marvel movie not only features Robert Downey Jr.'s Doctor Doom in a major way, but also brings back heroes from all corners of the franchise, and even includes players from Fox's X-Men universe and other live-action Marvel franchises.

Avengers: Doomsday is huge, destined to eclipse the $1 billion mark at the global box office, and all eyes are on how the MCU movie will pull off, including so many moving parts. To that end, a lot of fans have been getting their hopes up when it comes to Avengers: Doomsday's runtime, but a new report comes as a hard blow.

According to Omelete, Avengers: Doomsday has a runtime of 2 hours, 29 minutes, and 36 seconds in its standard version. As such, the new Marvel movie clocks in at 149 minutes, which is a match for 2018's Avengers: Infinity War, but falls below Avengers: Endgame's 185 minutes, aka over three hours. This is a somewhat disappointing development, as Avengers: Doomsday is such a massive event and has so many moving parts that it merits having a runtime of around three hours to make sure every character arc is as fleshed out as it can be and there are plenty of engaging fight sequences.

After all, this is not only the first Avengers movie of the Multiverse Saga, but it will have to deal with explaining the multiverse's collapse and feature several teams interacting for the first time, including the Fantastic Four, the Avengers, the New Avengers, the X-Men, and more. Finally, Doctor Doom's origin story and motivations will also need to be explained by the MCU movie, given the little build-up to the character beforehand, as will the return of Chris Evans' Steve Rogers, which an Avengers: Endgame Encore post-credits scene already began to explain. Doomsday is a huge movie, but its runtime falls below Eternals and others.

The most likely explanation for why Avengers: Doomsday's runtime clocks in under three hours is that the MCU is following the formula that resulted in its biggest Avengers movie wins. At the end of the Infinity Saga, Avengers: Infinity War was a more fast-paced and action-packed movie than 2019's Avengers: Endgame, and so it did not need to have as big a runtime as the latter. Given the urgency of the multiverse's collapse and how different heroes are trying to save their respective Earths, it makes sense for it to be fast-paced and pack a lot into its runtime.

With the upcoming Avengers movies expected to follow the comics, Doomsday could end with Downey's Doctor Doom emerging as the all-powerful God Emperor Doom and creating Battleworld. 2027's Avengers: Secret Wars could then have a slower pacing as the MCU heroes adapt to a new reality in a world made up of different multiverse locations and characters from all over Marvel lore, with a three-hour runtime similar to Endgame. That said, at first glance, Avengers: Doomsday's runtime does come as a bit of a disappointment.

20 Most Universally Loved TV Couples

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It's astonishing that romance series are considered niche, as almost everyone has opinions on the couples in the shows they watch. In fact, some of the most spirited TV discussions come from arguing about the best romance in a given series. It's been over 20 years since Buffy the Vampire Slayer ended, yet the Buffy-and-Angel vs. Buffy-and-Spike debates still rage on. Meanwhile, Gilmore Girls fans are no closer to reaching a consensus on Team Dean, Team Jess, or Team Logan than they were when the show aired in the 2000s.

But as fun as it is to put ourselves into staunch TV romance camps, it's just as wonderful when the stars align, and everyone can universally agree on small-screen pairings that are absolutely magical. This is no easy task — these relationships need enough ups and downs to keep audiences hooked, but not so toxic that they turn viewers off.

This can also change over time. Audiences who once eagerly tuned in to watch Ross and Rachel or Jim and Pam's relationship unfold have since reevaluated the couples and their romantic storylines as dysfunctional and grating. For a couple to be truly universally loved, they must still make their fans swoon.

The longest-running will-they, won't-they couple in television history, Olivia Benson and Elliot Stabler have been making audiences swoon for almost 30 years, all without ever having kissed — though they memorably came so close in Law & Order: SVU season 24. First meeting as partners in New York's Special Victims Unit, Benson and Stabler had instant rapport and crackling on-screen chemistry. Over the years, their loyalty and love for each other have only strengthened as they prove that sometimes the slowest-burning relationships are the hottest.

Most universally loved TV couples are popular because they're between two lovable people, but Blair and Chuck showed that you can still be a terrible person and have audiences root for your romance, because what's really important is being right for each other. Blair and Chuck are a perfect match in every way, from their raw ambition to their unbridled, animalistic mutual desire. One of Gossip Girl's harshest realities is that every character is actually awful, but these two scheming lovebirds make no apologies for it.

Forbidden love is always titillating, and it doesn't get much more forbidden than falling for a priest. However, while audiences may have been first hooked on the taboo element of Fleabag's season 2 romance, what really makes this relationship special is how vulnerable she and Hot Priest are with each other, thanks to their instant trust and connection. Going their separate ways in Fleabag's ending is so beautifully bittersweet because although they aren't together, their lives have been changed forever from having experienced the kind of love that they shared.

Willow and Tara were one of the first long-term Sapphic couples in television history, but their Buffy the Vampire Slayer romance is about so much more than breaking barriers. In a show full of toxic romances, these two stood out for forming a connection based on genuine warmth, affection, and empathy. Though this wasn't always maintained, as Willow's magic addiction temporarily tore them apart, it's clear that they belonged together, making Tara's "bury your gays" death one of the grimmest moments TV has ever seen.

Grand romantic gestures and tumultuous love triangles certainly make for great TV, but Janine and Greg show that in matters of the heart, it's often the little things that make the biggest difference, whether that's in drawing a couple together or keeping them apart. The Abbott Elementary characters' slow-burn road to romance was paved with small acts of kindness, like Greg being the only one to notice and compliment Janine's new hair, while their reasons for not leaping into a relationship were also grounded, like Janine needing time to be single as an adult. Ultimately, it all served to make their eventual, inevitable coupling feel earned.

Opposites-attract couples always have a fun frisson, but they carry extra emotional weight in teen dramas, where stepping outside your social circle and status is even more high-stakes. However, Seth and Summer's romance was so much deeper than just another nerd wooing the prom queen.

Teen dramas have always dominated television, but there are only a handful of titles that remain truly iconic in the age of modern streaming.

The O.C. characters' witty banter was bar none, and while it kept audiences entertained, what really made them root for these two as a couple was that they never stopped growing. Their wedding in The O.C.'s series finale was not only romantic but earned after four seasons of consistently being better for each other.

Parks and Rec is full of great couples, with pairings like Leslie and Ben or Ann and Chris also worthy candidates as one of TV's most universally loved couples. However, there's something even more special about two very different yet equally odd people not only appreciating but loving each other for their eccentricities. Because of this, April and Andy are free to be themselves, and their relationship is a wonderfully weird playground full of goofy inside jokes and alter egos. While they never take life too seriously, April's black-cat cynicism and Andy's golden-retriever enthusiasm help balance each other out.

Whereas many of TV's most famous LGBTQ+ couples have defining moments of tragedy, David Rose and Patrick Brewer are an ultimate celebration of queer joy. Though their relationship is hardly problem-free, it's always rooted in true love and pure happiness. Together, they are grounded and authentic, while their romantic moments, from Patrick publicly serenading David to their mountaintop engagement, are simply the best.

It's one thing to be a happy, healthy couple in the world of a low-stakes sitcom; it's another to maintain such a relationship when crises and trauma are an almost daily occurrence. However, Randall and Beth Pearson's marriage not only holds steadfast throughout six seasons of This Is Us, but it's a consistent bright spot in the tearjerking drama series. Throughout fostering and adopting a child, mental health struggles, and major career upheavals, the couple may experience friction, but they ultimately never lose their sense of playfulness, fizzy banter, or love for each other.

While Ross and Rachel were kept in perpetual will-they, won't-they purgatory throughout Friends' 10-season run, Monica and Chandler had a much more organic yet entertaining path from friends to lovers. What started as a shocking London hookup twist in season 4 grew into one of sitcom history's best partnerships thanks entirely to Courteney Cox and Matthew Perry's chemistry. Monica and Chandler experience huge struggles and don't always see eye to eye, but once they get together, there is never a question of whether they'll break up because it's clear that their love is so strong.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Vintage Modern’s New Fastback Turns the Mustang into a Carbon-Fiber Movie Star

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The classic look of the ‘Gone in 60 Seconds’ hero car, with modern underpinnings.

While there are seemingly unending restomod companies flooding into the scene every day now, Atlanta-based Vintage Modern is among the most established of them. Starting out seven years ago building a truck that looked like a vintage Bronco yet offered proper modern mechanicals underneath, the company has by now produced some 500 cars and delivered them to celebrities from Mark Wahlberg to LeBron James. Now, it’s expanding its range.

Speaking of celebrities, Vintage Modern’s new Mustang-based Fastback is clearly based on a certain 2000s movie star. The hero car from the Nicolas Cage movie Gone in 60 Seconds, a Chip Foose–customized 1967 Mustang GT500, is well known in the enthusiast community as “Eleanor,” the car Cage couldn’t keep his hands off.

See all results for used cars for sale near 60632

To be clear, Vintage Modern isn’t saying its carbon-fiber-bodied creation is a take on the Eleanor Mustang, but there are plenty of similarities up to and including those massive front lower driving lights. However, underneath things aren’t 1967 but 2026 and newer, with the platform of a modern S650-chassis Mustang providing the basis for the build.

Power starts from a standard 5.0-liter V-8 with 486 horsepower, paired with either a six-speed manual or a 10-speed automatic transmission. If you need more punch, perhaps for improbably enormous bridge jumps (professional driver, closed course), then a 3.0-liter Whipple supercharger ramps things up to a healthy 840 horsepower.

Overall, though, what you’re paying for with the Vintage Fastback is the build quality that comes with doing this kind of work for some time. Vintage Modern says that each car represents 900 hours of assembly time, with hand-laid carbon-fiber panels and handcrafted assembly.

No cost is mentioned at this point, though a quick look at what the Modern Classic truck costs indicates that those builds start at around the $250,000 mark. Since everything’s handmade apart from the mechanical bits, there’s an enormous amount of customization available, so you’re free to keep tacking on line items to that price.

Spending that kind of money on a Mustang is a certain kind of flex, but the Fastback’s blend of movie star looks and dependable modern underpinnings should be tempting for those with deep pockets and large Instagram followings. Just maybe lock the keys in a safe if you’re having Nic Cage over for lunch.

Rob Lowe Asked Ben Shapiro to Cast Him in ‘Confession’: “I’ve Worked With Left-Wing Writers. Why Wouldn’t I Work With Right-Wing Writers?”

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Daily Wire founder recently said he told the actor he was "way too handsome" to take on the role.

Rob Lowe was ready to sign on to star in Ben Shapiro‘s Confession after he read the “fantastic” script written by the Daily Wire founder.

The Emmy nominee went on the Second Thought podcast Tuesday, where he recounted how he got involved with the film. He started, “I read the script; it was written by Ben Shapiro. I called Ben and said, ‘This is fantastic.’ And he was like, ‘Well, do you think you know anybody 50-ish, leading man who would not be scared just because I wrote it?’ And I go, ‘Yeah, me.’”

“And he goes, ‘You’re good with this?’ I said, ‘I’ve worked with the most left-wing writers in the world. Why wouldn’t I also work with the most right-wing writers in the world?’” Lowe added. Related Stories Movies 'Confession' Review: Rob Lowe and James Franco in a Ben Shapiro-Scripted, Stalinist Russia-Set Drama That's Terminally Tedious Midterms 2026 Ben Shapiro Says 2026 Midterms Were "A Wipeout for Republicans": "The President Was Kind of on the Ballot"

During a recent appearance on the Conversations With Coleman podcast on Saturday, Shapiro said that the actor called him and said he was “deeply affected by the script.” When he asked if he could play the lead part, Shapiro said he replied by saying, “You’re Rob Lowe. If you ask to play a part, the answer is yes, but I’m just telling you you’re way too handsome for this. Like, you’re way too good-looking for this part.”

Of Confession, Lowe said the movie was “turned down by every single film festival.” He added, “Summarily frog-marched out of any conversation. Now, this is a movie with me, Nick Nolte, James Franco, Emile Hirsch. I see what they’re picking up, and it’s all political.”

“The thought would be we’d play in a film festival, we would get some buzz, never happened. And then you know Netflix would pick it up or this one would pick — crickets. Absolute crickets,” Lowe said. “So then it defaults to the Daily Wire to be the distributor, and I’m like, hell yeah. I want people to see this movie. And good for them for putting it out. But that wasn’t originally going to be the life of the movie.”

Confession was directed by Václav Marhoul and centers on the interrogation of a prisoner in a Stalin-era Russian jail.

Sony’s impressive Bravia 8 II OLED hits a new low price ahead of Black Friday

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Black Friday is a great time to save on a new OLED television for your winter movie nights. Though, it turns out that you can already get a great deal on the 65-inch Sony Bravia 8 II, one of our favorite OLED televisions. It’s currently discounted by $1,100 at various retailers, including Amazon, Best Buy, and directly from Sony. That discount brings the price under $2,200, the lowest we’ve seen. You can also save $800 on the 55-inch model, which is typically $2,599.99 (Amazon, Best Buy, Sony), but now costs just under $1,800.

The Bravia 8 II was chosen as the “king of TV” by a panel of judges (including The Verge’s own Nilay Patel) in Value Electronics’ 2025 shootout. The OLED TV has excellent color accuracy, a bright panel, and a 120Hz refresh rate.

My colleague John Higgins praised the Bravia 8 II for its excellent image quality and engaging picture, though those positive traits are consistent with almost all OLED TVs. What sets this one apart is its impressive image processing, the bold colors from its quantum dot-equipped OLED panel, and that it looks great right out of the box (although he recommends using Professional mode). The screen was awarded the top spot at 2025’s Value Electronics TV Shootout, beating out similar offerings from competitors like Panasonic, LG, and Samsung.

For gaming, the panel can reach up to 120Hz at 4K, with both variable refresh rate and automatic low-latency mode to ensure that your content looks and feels good to play. The connectivity includes two HDMI 2.1 (one with eARC), and two HDMI 2.0 ports, giving you plenty of inputs for consoles and a Blu-ray player, among other gadgets. We spotted some color fringing of certain colors in dark scenes, and lamented the lack of a but it’s an otherwise excellent screen we can easily recommend.

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Taylor Sheridan has had a lot going on this year, but that isn’t all that surprising to those who have been following his work since the early days of Yellowstone. There are also those who have been around for his projects since before he ever wrote an episode of Yellowstone — back in 2015, Sheridan made his feature writing debut on Sicario, the neo-Western crime thriller starring Josh Brolin and Emily Blunt. After writing the 2018 Sicario sequel, Day of the Soldado, Sheridan is not involved with the third Sicario movie that was confirmed a few weeks ago. Steven Knight, writer of Peaky Blinders and the new Robert Pattinson-led James Bond movie in the works at Amazon, is writing the script for the film with Padraic McKinley (The Weight) set to step behind the camera and direct.

The most exciting project on the horizon for Sheridan is, without a doubt, the third season of Landman. It was thought that Landman Season 3 would make it back to Paramount Plus before the end of the year, but several production delays led to the show being pushed back to 2027. However, cameras on Landman Season 3 are rolling, with new cast members such as Michael Kelly (Lioness) being announced on almost a weekly basis. This afternoon, the cast of the show grew even further, when news broke this afternoon that Scott Speedman, best known for starring in R.J. Decker and Running Point, has officially joined the cast of Taylor Sheridan's Landman Season 3. Details about his role are being kept under wraps for now. No word yet on if his guest starring role will be limited to one episode or multiple.

The show that claimed the most of your answers is the world you were built for. If two tied, both are shown — you're complicated enough to straddle two Sheridan universes.

You are a Dutton — or you might as well be. You understand that some things are worth protecting at any cost, and that the modern world's indifference to history, to land, to legacy, is not something you're willing to accept quietly. You lead from the front, you carry your family's weight without complaint, and when someone threatens what's yours, you don't escalate — you finish it. You're not cruel. But you are absolute. In Yellowstone's world, that combination of ferocity and loyalty doesn't make you a villain. It makes you the only thing standing between everything that matters and everyone who wants to take it.

You thrive in the chaos of high-stakes negotiation, where the money is enormous, the margins are thin, and the wrong word in the wrong room can cost everyone everything. You're a fixer — the person called when a situation is already on fire and needs someone with the nerve to walk into it. West Texas oil country rewards exactly what you are: sharp, adaptable, unsentimental, and absolutely clear-eyed about what people want and what they'll do to get it. You're not naive enough to think this world is fair. You're smart enough to be the one deciding who it's fair to.

You are a Dwight Manfredi — someone who has served their time, paid their dues, and arrived somewhere unexpected with nothing but their reputation and their wits. You adapt without losing yourself. You build loyalty through respect rather than fear, though you're not above reminding people that the two aren't mutually exclusive. Tulsa King is for people who are still standing when everyone assumed they'd be finished — who find, in an unfamiliar place, that they're more capable than the world gave them credit for. You don't need a throne. You build one, wherever you happen to land.

You carry the weight of a system that is broken by design, and you do it anyway — because someone has to, and because you're the only one positioned to do it without the whole thing collapsing. Mike McLusky's world is for people who are comfortable operating where there are no good options, only less catastrophic ones. You speak every language: law enforcement, criminal, political, human. That fluency makes you invaluable and it makes you a target. You've made your peace with both. Mayor of Kingstown belongs to people who understand that keeping the peace is not the same as being at peace — and who do the job regardless.

Heading into Landman Season 3, most major cast members such as Ali Larter, Demi Moore, and even Michelle Randolph are receiving huge pay bumps for their roles in the third season. The only cast member returning and not receiving a raise is Billy Bob Thornton, who stars in the leading role as Tommy Norris. Thornton isn’t eligible for a raise yet because he originally inked a three-season deal to star in the show. Should it be renewed for Season 3, he’s in line for a massive payday.

Netflix's Western Mystery Thriller Heartland Debuts To 13.9 Million Hours Viewed

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Though Netflix's original films span several genres, its Westerns are much sparser, ranging from gritty frontier dramas, comedies, neo-Westerns, and revenge thrillers. They include The Ridiculous 6 (2015), a slapstick comedy starring Adam Sandler; the Coen Brothers' Ballad of Buster Scruggs (2018), a quirky anthology; The Harder They Fall (2021), a stylized film starring Jonathan Majors and Idris Elba as rival outlaws; and Jane Campion's The Power of the Dog (2021), a drama starring Benedict Cumberbatch as a domineering ranch owner in 1920s Montana.

Now, Netflix has a new Western mystery thriller, Heartland. Written and directed by Shana Feste, the movie stars Oscar winner Jessica Chastain as former country megastar Misty Jones, who comes out of seclusion to return to Nashville and investigate the disappearance of her niece, a rising country star, and must grapple with her own past and the Music City's seedy underbelly. The cast also includes Garrett Hedlund, Carter Faith, Ross Lynch, Paul Sparks, Jennifer Nettles, and John Hawkes.

Released on November 13, Heartland has debuted at No. 1 on Netflix's Global Top 10 movies with 13.9 million hours viewed and 7.3 million total views for the week of November 9–15. It finished above A Dog's Perfect Christmas, The Gospel of Christmas, Reminders of Him, Allen, Sing 2, How the Grinch Stole Christmas, Funny Money: The Dane Cook Story, All the Pretty Horses, and Sing.

Despite debuting No. 1 on Netflix last week, Heartland has received mixed reviews, resulting in a 61% Rotten Tomatoes score from critics, coupled with a 42% audience score. While critics praise Jessica Chastain's commanding performance, the country music atmosphere, and occasional thrills, they also take issue with its predictable plot, sluggish pacing, clunky writing, and lack of emotional depth.

In ScreenRant's Heartland review, Alex Harrison rated the film five out of 10 stars and writes, "Jessica Chastain keeps [the] middling Netflix thriller watchable... There's a really good drama somewhere in Jessica Chastain's country music thriller, but Heartland buries it underneath a rote mystery story." He continues:

Heartland won't be living rent-free in your mind anytime soon, but while it's on, it is most certainly watchable.

On the other hand, audience reactions are also mixed, with some enjoying Chastain's performance, the country music, and the entertaining mystery, while others criticize its occasional cheesy acting, predictable storyline, weak writing, and underwhelming thriller elements. Ultimately, Heartland may not be Netflix's greatest Western, but Chastain gives audiences enough reason to saddle up and give the intriguing mystery a chance.

Sebastian Stan’s New Thriller Officially Removed From 500 Theaters

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

While some Oscar contenders have emerged as massive box-office hits already this year, others have struggled to break out of niche circles. Two films at opposite ends of this spectrum are currently playing in theaters, having delivered drastically different results. The three-hour documentary You Can See Everything has defied the odds to gross nearly $100 million at the domestic box office, and thanks to the film's incredible reception, A24 is gearing up to campaign for a Best Picture nomination. On the other end of the spectrum is Neon's seventh consecutive Palme d'Or winner, which is on its way out of theaters after a so-so box office run.

The movie premiered at the Cannes Film Festival earlier this year, where it won the festival's top honor, continuing Neon's incredible streak. The distributor has picked up the American rights to seven Palme d'Or winners in a row — Parasite, Titane, Triangle of Sadness, Anatomy of a Fall, Anora, and It Was Just an Accident. Two of these movies — Parasite and Anora — ended up winning the Best Picture and Best Director Oscars.

It remains to be seen if Neon's latest can score a Best Picture nod as well, although it has already been selected as Romania's official entry in the Best International Feature category. The movie was directed by Romanian auteur Cristian Mungiu, who now has two Palme d'Ors to his name. It stars Sebastian Stan and Renate Reinsve in the lead roles of a married couple whose conservatism attracts attention when they move to a progressive Norwegian town.

We're talking, of course, about Fjord. Unlike Parasite, which grossed more than $50 million domestically, Fjord has so far struggled at the box office. The movie has generated $1.8 million domestically after nearly 40 days of release, during which its peak theater count stood at 540. The film is now playing in just 44 locations nationwide.

The film received positive reviews and now sits at a "Certified Fresh" 92% score on the aggregator website Rotten Tomatoes. The site's consensus reads, "Presenting a knotty moral dilemma and refusing to provide easy answers, Cristian Mungiu's superbly well-acted Fjord has a chilly surface that belies a heated humanity beneath the frost." Reinsve is coming off the blockbuster horror film Backrooms, which grossed $400 million worldwide, while Stan will reprise his role as the Winter Soldier in the upcoming Avengers: Doomsday.

Fjord follows the Gheorghius, a Romanian-Catholic family who move to a remote Norwegian village hoping to build a quieter life. As they grow close to their neighbors, the Halbergs, cultural differences and local suspicion begin to turn that welcome into hostility, leading to accusations that threaten both families and expose the fragile line between trust and fear.

‘Grendel’ Animated Series Set at Netflix From ‘Widow’s Bay’ Writer Years After Live-Action Adaptation Was Scrapped

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Netflix has greenlit an adult animated series based on “Grendel,” the popular Dark Horse comic book series by Matt Wagner. Serving as showrunner and executive producer is Bobak Esfarjani, who has previously written and produced on “Widow’s Bay,” “Alien: Earth,” “WandaVision” and more.

The news comes four years after the streamer scrapped a live-action “Grendel” adaptation it had ordered with Andrew Dabb writing and Abubakr Ali playing the title role. Related Stories Fox News Sets Midterm Elections Coverage: Here's How to Stream 'Democracy 26' Online Free Alycia Baumgardner vs. Elif Nur Turhan on ESPN Unlimited: Here’s Where to Watch the Title Bout Live Online

The official logline for the new “Grendel” series reads, “After the woman he loves is shockingly murdered, Hunter Rose devotes himself to one thing: vengeance. But as he tears through street gangs, the elite and New York City’s supernatural underworld in pursuit of her killer, a chance at a real life forces him to choose: walk away from ultimate power, or become the very monster he hunts.”

Alongside Esfarjani, executive producers include Chris Tongue, Tim Wiesch and Jay Komas for Dark Horse Entertainment; Lydia Antonini; and Wagner. Bobbypills is the studio.

“It’s incredibly exciting to see my creation finally make this leap to the animated screen!” Wagner said in a statement. “Netflix’s dynamic new series retains the source material’s dark adult themes and also brings a fresh and thrilling approach to the narrative. This is ‘Grendel’ as I’ve always wanted to see the character portrayed — a hyper-kinetic level of action, bracing explosions of violence and a poignant engaging storyline.”

“‘Grendel’ has been at the forefront of independent comics for over forty years, and we’re thrilled to bring Matt Wagner’s creation to Netflix,” said John Derderian, vice president of animation series and kids and family TV at Netflix. “Like the comic, this animated series will break the traditional superhero mold just as Matt’s time-honored comic book has always done. Bobak, Dark Horse Entertainment and Bobbypills are bold storytellers who love this world as much as its fans do, and we’re so excited to partner with them to bring this story to screen.”

“Being able to bring a character as iconic as ‘Grendel’ to life has been a true thrill,” said Esfarjani. “Having the support of Matt Wagner, my great EPs and the awesome team of Netflix has made it all the more exciting, and partnering with Bobbypills Studio has made it all a true joy.”

2026’s Best-Sci Fi Movie Is Officially Returning to IMAX

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

You have another chance to catch Project Hail Mary on the biggest screen possible

Sometimes a movie's spectacle is so great that it really does need to be experienced in a theater, preferably in the best possible format available to you. Project Hail Mary, this year's stunning Andy Weir adaptation starring Ryan Gosling, is one such movie. It may have been difficult to catch in IMAX, however, as IMAX screens are a hot commodity and the film has been out of theaters for a while. If you missed it the first time around, Amazon MGM Studios has an end-of-year treat for you.

As announced on the film's official X account, Project Hail Mary will return to theaters​​​​​​​ in all formats this December. That includes IMAX and 70mm​​​​​​​. The special event begins on Dec. 11, but it's not clear if it's a one-day-only event or if Project Hail Mary will have IMAX screens up until Dune: Part 3's release on Dec. 18.

The film adaptation of Andy Weir's 2021 novel​​​​​​​ of the same name follows Ryland Grace (Gosling) as he wakes up on a spaceship without his memory. Grace encounters the alien Rocky and the two embark on a shared mission to save their species, while Grace slowly begins to recall the events that led him to go on the mission in the first place.

Project Hail Mary became an instant critical and commercial success when it was released earlier this year. Much of that success was due to​​​​​​​ Gosling's heartwarming performance and the incredible puppetry behind Rocky. The film has already garnered some awards buzz, and its December theatrical re-release — right around when awards voters begin considering end-of-year-awards contenders — is a smart move.​​​​​​​ Of course, the movie is also currently streaming on Amazon Prime Video if you want to stay in the comfort of your own home.

If Gosling manages to secure an Oscar nomination for Project Hail Mary, which is a big if, then it'll come ahead of another big sci-fi turn for the actor. Next year, Gosling is set to star in Star Wars: Starfighter, which is helmed by Deadpool & Wolverine director Shawn Levy. The film is set to take place five years after the events of The Rise of Skywalker, which means it will go into some uncharted territory for the franchise. The movie is slated to be released on May 28, 2027.

After that, Gosling is set to team up​​​​​​​ with Levy once again in 2028 for Marvel's Ghost Rider reboot. After years of openly saying he wanted the role, Gosling's casting was finally revealed at San Diego Comic-Con​​​​​​​. It is also rumored that Heated Rivalry breakout Connor Storrie will play the film's villain. Gosling sure is busy.

Inside Well Beyond Breast Cancer’s Fundraiser to Give Survivors Access to Rehabilitation

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Many don’t realize that breast cancer care doesn’t end with treatment.

Under violet lights, hanging florals, and rattan lanterns, guests at Well Beyond Breast Cancer’s third annual fundraising event mingled, cried happy tears, and came together in New York City to support survivors. Well Beyond Breast Cancer is a nonprofit organization that helps survivors access rehabilitation services after breast cancer treatment. Founder Amanda Grilli, an oncology physical therapist, decided to start the organization just three years ago after moving to the United States from Canada and recognizing a major gap in care.

“I was shocked by the differences in the health care system between the two countries,” Grilli told ELLE. “I quickly realized that the majority of individuals who are affected by breast cancer don’t have access to a therapist like myself because they’re experiencing so many barriers, whether that’s financial toxicity, insurance gaps, or a fragmented health care system. [Many] people aren’t able to access this care on their own.”

For breast cancer survivors, care continues even after surgery, chemotherapy, or radiation end. But rehabilitation is a major piece of breast cancer recovery that often isn’t considered. Grilli says, “So many people experience lingering pain, or they have difficulty lifting their arms. What these therapists are doing is helping people overcome these side effects so that they can get back to living well.”

Guests had the opportunity to hear from one of the organization’s beneficiaries, Allison Berg, an independent documentary filmmaker who was diagnosed with breast cancer in 2025. Even after treatment, her recovery continued. Berg described shooting pain through her arms and fingertips, tightness every time she reached for anything, and tender scars. “My body is part of my equipment,” she shared. “I was having trouble just sitting at a laptop and typing. How was I ever going to carry gear and hold a camera again?” Well Beyond Breast Cancer covered 12 one-hour rehabilitation sessions for Berg, and in September, she was able to shoot for a new film.

Prior to the event yesterday, Well Beyond Breast Cancer had funded 250 breast cancer rehab appointments and allocated more than $50,000 to direct patient care. Last night, at ILA Penthouse in Manhattan, guests were able to participate in a live auction, which is still ongoing and has already raised thousands of dollars. Before they left, guests were also invited to share a wish with the “Keeper of Wishes,” a figurehead with “hopes” pinned to her dress, creating a growing display and fountain of hope that continued throughout the night.

Katie Berohn is ELLE’s beauty editor. Previously, she held the same title at Who What Wear, where she was promoted from associate beauty editor. She’s written for publications like The Cut, Cosmopolitan, Good Housekeeping, and Mashable. Her interests include fragrance, vintage shopping, hot yoga, food, travel, music, books, and attempting to make every NYT Cooking recipe. She’s on the endless hunt to find the perfect shade of red lipstick.

When the season expands to 18 games, reseeding will likely follow

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

With the NFC South destined to send a none-of-the-above team to the playoffs and to have that team host a playoff game, there inevitably will be criticism of the current structure of the postseason. And it inevitably will dust off the proposal the Lions made two years ago, with a nudge from the league office, to reseed the seven teams based on record and not on division championship.

When will the talk become action? When the NFL expands to regular season to 18 games.

That’s the current thinking, as we’ve consistently heard it. Expansion of the regular season will result in reseeding of the playoffs,

Winning the division will still get a team in. But whether that team hosts a playoff game will depend on whether its record is one of the four best in the conference.

The best approach would be to realign the league to four eight-team conferences. Four division champions, 10 wild-cards. No arbitrary separation of AFC and NFC.

We won’t hold our breath for that one to happen. The league takes baby steps, not giant leaps. And when it takes its next step to 18 games, wild-card teams likely will be able to leap lesser division champions.

Level Up Your Next Family Ski Trip at Banff Sunshine Village

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Sunshine Ski Village in Banff, Canada. (Photo: Sunshine Ski Village)

One of the best things about skiing with kids is that your definition of a great day changes. It stops being about vertical feet, powder depth, or how many black diamonds you can check off before lunch. Instead, it’s about finding a mountain where everyone can have fun together today while still having room to grow tomorrow.

That’s what makes Banff Sunshine Village such a gem for families. What really sets this ski destination  apart is the balance between beginner, intermediate, advanced, and expert terrain. All levels of skiers have plenty of runs to explore, making it easy for families of mixed abilities to spend the day on the mountain together. Across three connected mountains and more than 3,500 acres of terrain, there’s room to return season after season as confidence grows and skills evolve.

For many families, the sweet spot starts on the lower mountain around the Wolverine and Jackrabbit chairs. These areas feel like they were designed by people who know what it’s like to ski with enthusiastic kids. Runs like Miss Gratz, Rollercoaster, Kids Play, Wolverine, and Blue Bell offer the perfect mix of gentle pitches, playful rollers, natural features, and kid-approved tree skiing. Instead of simply sliding down groomers, young skiers and riders get to explore terrain that quietly teaches balance, turning, and confidence while they’re busy having fun.

That natural terrain approach creates a progression that feels effortless. One season you have your kids between your own skis, the next they’re asking to duck into the trees or tackle steeper lines.

As abilities advance, Sunshine keeps delivering. Families ready to explore more challenging terrain can tap into Banff Sunshine’s Ski & Snowboard School, where lessons help skiers and riders build confidence well beyond the beginner stage. For those interested in freeride skills, specialized instruction and the resort’s beacon training area provide a chance to learn mountain safety fundamentals in a controlled environment before taking on bigger adventures.

The secret weapon might be staying slopeside at the Sunshine Mountain Lodge. Instead of ending the day with a drive back to town, families can stretch the adventure into the evening. Tobogganing, movie nights, hot tub bingo, and nightly s’mores give kids something to look forward to after the lifts stop spinning, while parents get a chance to relax and enjoy being fully immersed in mountain life.

The best family ski destinations aren’t the ones you outgrow. They’re the ones that grow with you. At Banff Sunshine, that’s exactly what happens.

Banff Sunshine Village is the premier ski and snowboard resort in the Canadian Rockies. It’s famed for being home of Canada’s Best Snow – light, fluffy, smoke-dry powder and for having the longest non-glacier season in Canada. The resort is typically open from early November until late May. Guests of Sunshine can elevate their Canadian Rocky Mountain experience by staying at Sunshine Mountain Lodge, the Banff National Park’s only ski in, ski out hotel.

Ex-Trump Adviser Jason Miller Accused of Harassing Baby Mama in Deposition, Attorney Calls BS

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

One of Donald Trump's top campaign advisers is facing some nasty allegations from his baby mama ... who claims he turned a recent deposition into a personal attack -- but his attorney says it's all BS.

According to court documents obtained by TMZ, Jason Miller's former flame, A.J. Delgado, claims he repeatedly harassed and insulted her during a November 9 deposition in their ongoing child support battle ... eventually pushing her to cut the questioning short.

Delgado alleges Miller referred to her as a "complete whack job," a "deranged person," and a "sick individual" during the deposition ... while also calling her a stalker and a security threat to his family.

She says she tried to keep things professional, but Miller allegedly continued laughing, texting on his phone, and refusing to answer basic questions about his finances.

Delgado also takes aim at the magistrate overseeing the deposition ... claiming he failed to rein Miller in and refused her request to contact the judge assigned to the case.

She says things got so heated she ended the deposition with roughly half her questions unanswered ... claiming she couldn't endure any more of the alleged verbal abuse.

But Miller's attorney, Sandy T. Fox, says Delgado's claims have absolutely no merit. Fox tells us ... “Since 2017, Ms. Delgado has repeatedly made false allegations against Mr. Miller, his counsel, members of the judiciary and those who are not part of her narrative."

The lawyer went on ... "Her conduct during Mr. Miller’s deposition is just part and parcel of her repeated abusive litigation conduct, which resulted in members of the judiciary questioning her mental health and entering orders prohibiting abusive litigation conduct."

Fox added, "Her request for “additional pages” as a result of purported allegations that took place during a deposition that she voluntarily terminated was denied, as was her request to stay the trial court proceedings to delay the trial and continue the final hearing.

As TMZ previously reported, Miller and Delgado welcomed a son in 2017 after having an affair while working on Trump's 2016 presidential campaign. Miller -- who mysteriously resigned as communications director before the '17 inauguration -- was married at the time, and their relationship has since sparked years of bitter legal battles over their child.

And the courtroom drama is nothing new ... Miller reportedly took aim at a judge overseeing their paternity dispute in a social media post earlier this year.

‘Lanterns’ Star Officially Moves on With New Netflix Action Thriller

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

In 2011, Netflix secured exclusive movie streaming rights with Lionsgate for the UK and Ireland. In the decade and a half that has followed, the relationship between the two companies has grown. With the rise of Netflix as arguably the biggest streaming service on the planet in the age of the streaming wars, Lionsgate has also benefited from having such close ties. Earlier this year, the duo enjoyed extraordinary success after their collaboration on War Machine, starring Alan Ritchson in the lead role, premiered. It was not the first time Netflix and Lionsgate had undertaken such a project, with Jingle Bell Heist (starring Olivia Holt and Connor Swindells) and Dhamaka, prior examples.

A sequel to War Machine is officially in the works, which will bring back Ritchson, best known for playing the title role in Reacher, as an all-action hero. Now, that relationship is set to yield even more fruit, as both companies are teaming up once more on a new action thriller, Fairtytale In New York. After Netflix won a competitive auction, the streaming service will distribute the picture with Lionsgate producing. Fairtytale In New York will hope to capture some of the magic that made War Machine such a success, and they might well be on the right track with this key decision.

Jalmari Helander is attached to direct the action thriller, but it's the film's lead actor that is the main draw. Fresh off another action-heavy role with his hit HBO series Lanterns, actor Aaron Pierre will lead the effort for Fairtytale In New York, per Deadline. Pierre co-starred alongside Kyle Chandler in the sci-fi series, which was one of HBO Max's biggest dramas of 2026. The upcoming action thriller is a reunion of sorts, as it reunites Sisu director Helander with not only Lionsgate, but also screenwriters Rory Haines and Sohrab Noshirvani, who helped pen Helander's upcoming John Rambo project starring Noah Centineo and premiering on June 4, 2027.

After two prior collaborations with Helander, Sisu and Sisu: Road to Revenge, Lionsgate Motion Group President Erin Westerman has praised the director's ability to deliver visceral action without losing sight of other aspects along the way. Westerman also sees Pierre as a strong match for what they are seeking to deliver with Fairtytale In New York. "This marks our third collaboration with Jalmari. He’s an exceptional filmmaker with a distinctive ability to deliver visceral, memorable action while never losing sight of character and emotion. That makes him a perfect match for Aaron Pierre, who has all the makings of a major movie star — we think this is going to be a very special film.”

Fairtytale In New York is set on Christmas Eve in New York City, where an unassuming cabbie embarks on a relentless night of vengeance after his estranged son’s priceless gift is stolen.

Can <i>Rocky</i> Save the World?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Anthony Ippolito (Sylvester Stallone) in I Play Rocky

Sylvester Stallone has been a Hollywood institution for so long — his breakthrough movie Rocky premiered 50 years ago — that his previous underdog status as a struggling actor seems perfectly mythic in the new behind-the-scenes biographical drama I Play Rocky.

The fairy-tale aspect of I Play Rocky comes from its background story. In this, young Stallone (played by Anthony Ippolito) is a virtually homeless outsider, working at a movie theater, who after spending 16 months on writing eight screenplays, joins the creative zeitgeist of 1970s Hollywood, also known as the American Renaissance. It is this personal journey that illuminates the Stallone legend that climaxes with Rocky winning the Academy Award for best picture over such esteemed movies as Taxi Driver, Network, Bound for Glory, and All the President’s Men.

Starting with Stallone practicing elocution (using Walt Whitman’s “I Sing the Body Electric”) to soften his rough New York accent, the film then places him against odds in an era of cultural ferment that included such films as Altman’s The Long Goodbye and Coppola’s The Conversation. (Stallone pays particular attention to the intonation of Coppola’s actors repeating the line “He’d kill us if he found out.”) Screenwriter Peter Gamble doesn’t make that detail ominous but simplifies the life-or-death aspect of Stallone’s career and artistic ambition as part of the wannabe actor’s desperate, working-class consciousness — inspired by viewing the heavyweight championship boxing match between Muhammad Ali and Chuck Wepner. It’s easier to take than the sociological theme in Green Book, the egregious previous film of director Peter Farrelly. I Play Rocky regains the genial humanism of such Farrelly brothers’ movies as Me, Myself and Irene, Stuck on You, and The Ringer, and especially the Farrelly brothers’ script for Outside Providence.

Kiki Seto (Talia Shire) and Anthony Ippolito (Sylvester Stallone) in I Play Rocky

Stallone’s ambition is epitomized by his insistence that the Rocky screenplay he wrote in four days and sold to producers Irwin Winkler and Robert Chartoff can be filmed only starring himself. He demands, “I play Rocky.” That’s not a playground taunt in the milieu of financial bargaining and egotism that’s gleefully depicted by a series of Hollywood types — P. J. Byrne and Toby Kebbell as Winkler and Chartoff, Rob Demery as Joe Frazier, and a standout cameo by Trevor St. John as a self-serious fight coordinator.

In Hollywood’s gladiatorial circus, Stallone’s striving and eventual success become a credible moral victory. He must claim his own identity and career path separately from his worldly father Frank (Matt Dillon gives a moving characterization of a parent’s cynicism, affection, and personal regret). His wife Sasha (AnnaSophie Robb) encourages him: “I’d rather be optimistic and be wrong than pessimistic and be right.” These backstage details (including a hostile producer who dismisses Stallone as “a droopy-eyed hack actor”) make I Play Rocky a credible and appealing saga of Stallone’s indefatigable confidence. The movie rejects the sort of dark, objectionable narcissism that we saw in the Springsteen hagiography Deliver Me from Nowhere.

It is important that the filmmakers accurately measure Rocky’s significance. Farrelly and Gamble mix film history with good sense and humor when portraying wild-eyed director John Avildsen (Jay Duplass) deep in his own world of pragmatic professionalism (“Great art never comes through compromise”). They also capture Stallone’s own, uncanny sense of achieving the proper effect — not so much when Rocky emotes to his manager Mickey as when, in a startling shot, he points to details in a big-screen image of Kirk Douglas in Champion, from 1948.

Putting Rocky in proper perspective is a curatorial feat. It recognizes Stallone’s film as a work of pop lore. Its popularity gave Hollywood the opportunity to ignore scary powerful art like Scorsese’s Taxi Driver in favor of the folk and commercial elements that are easily enjoyed in American culture — although missing from Millennial film culture. Remember: In 1976, Rocky seemed an artificial retread of old boxing films, pilfering the ethnic sentimentality of Marty, On the Waterfront, and Requiem for a Heavyweight as if Muhammad Ali had never happened.

But I Play Rocky works differently. It recalls Stallone’s response to Wepner (“This guy won’t go down!”) to achieve the authentic Millennial pop lore of Better Man, Song Sung Blue, Back 2 America, Power Ballad, and the upcoming Madden. (These restorative myths correct the disappointing Creed sequels that failed to live up to Rocky.) I Play Rocky channels Stallone’s personal perseverance to enlighten our current demoralized sensibilities.

Armond White is National Review’s culture critic and the author of New Position: The Prince Chronicles and Make Spielberg Great Again: The Steven Spielberg Chronicles.
